# Klatch Attention Rollup

**Purpose:** the document xian skims to know what asks of him *first*. Demand-organized, sorted by what each item asks of him — not by topic. (Per Exec 2026-06-19.) **Also consumed by Janus's federated cross-project rollup** — keep it accurate and current.

**Anti-bottleneck function** (xian, 2026-06-19): the rollup makes 1:1s start primed rather than in catch-up. xian still works directly with each agent — it makes those conversations productive, it doesn't replace them.

**Trust-instrument discipline** (Exec 2026-06-19): every render comes from a fresh **verified sweep** of source docs — never from memory. A false "all clear" is a trust breach. "Quiet" must mean *verified-clear*, not *haven't-checked*.

**Last refreshed:** 2026-09-04, STOP fire (Calliope) — v102. **The browse-latency cap ruling now has a measured price tag, and the multipart-upload objection to raising the import-size cap is gone — the real cost of an accepted upload is 9×, not the buffering the cap was thought to guard.**

Two things landed since v101, both verification against decisions already made, neither reopening either. **(1) Daedalus measured the multipart upload path Round 150 had left unmeasured, and found the size cap was never guarding what it looked like it guarded.** `c.req.formData()` reads the whole request body before any handler check runs, so refusing an over-cap file by the size check (329 ms, 169.6 MB peak) costs the same as refusing it one line earlier by the `.jsonl` extension check (277 ms, 170.5 MB peak) — indistinguishable, because nothing in the handler could have prevented that allocation either way. Shipped `rejectOversizeBeforeRead(c)` at all four multipart sites, refusing on `Content-Length` before `formData()`: the same rejection now costs 95–109 ms at 0.0 MB, at any cap value. **What the cap actually bounds, and still does:** an *accepted* upload costs **~9× the file** in peak memory (45.3 MB file → 419.2 MB peak) — two full copies plus a UTF-16 string before parsing starts. At the current 50 MB cap that's ~460 MB worst case; admitting PM's 70.3 MB `docs` head would put it near 650 MB. This changes the shape of the open Backfill 🔴 decision below: raising `MAX_IMPORT_SIZE` no longer trades against upload-time memory safety on rejection (that's now free), it trades against accepted-upload memory cost, which scales with the cap either way. **(2) The Round 147/148 cache-cold browse discrepancy Daedalus and Theseus each flagged "unexplained" is closed — it was the cap ruling itself, mid-air.** Daedalus measured cache-cold browse at 1477 ms before `18d4631` shipped (09:23); Theseus measured 2164 ms after (post-10:18) — same endpoint, same corpus, same discipline, different build. Reproduced both figures directly (1492 ms at cap 1500, 2203 ms at cap 50,000, three fresh servers, constant rewritten and sha256-restored): **the cap ruling costs 723 ms per cache-cold browse (48% over pre-ruling), paid once per server start** — the fingerprint cache absorbs the entire difference after the first browse (8 ms warm, either cap) — **and it bought 59.4% of the corpus's turn signal**, independently reproducing Round 143's 58.8% from a different instrument at a different layer. Recording the price of a decision already made, not reopening it. Metrics strip unchanged at 3 — both items sharpen numbers under already-counted open items rather than opening new ones. Full detail: `docs/import-multipart-cap-2026-09-04.md`, `docs/browse-cold-figure-gap-2026-09-04.md`, `daedalus-to-theseus-janus-iris-cc-calliope-argus-xian-the-cap-never-guarded-the-upload-path-and-now-it-does-2026-09-04.md`, `theseus-to-daedalus-cc-janus-iris-calliope-argus-xian-cold-figure-gap-closed-it-was-the-cap-2026-09-04.md`.

**Prior banner (v101, superseded):** The browse-latency cap is decided (xian, 9/4: raised 1500 → 50,000) — but Friday's real corpus just got bigger, and a second, un-ruled cap now blocks three of its eleven files outright. Three things landed that fire. **(1) The cap ruling.** Daedalus shipped `FINGERPRINT_LINE_CAP` 1500 → 50,000 as a pathological-file guard rather than a latency knob, retiring the `turnCount`/`messageCount+` rendering question Iris was holding for — full ruling moved to ✅ below. **(2) The scanner can now see Piper Morgan's actual corpus, and it isn't the six-department-head, 72-channel March snapshot the Backfill section has been circling since 8/12 — it's eleven live, currently-growing role sessions in a second Claude config directory (`~/.claude-pm`) that the scanner was silently blind to.** `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` (relocate) plus a new `KLATCH_EXTRA_SESSION_ROOTS` (additive) fixed that — union is 593 sessions at 9 ms warm — and xian confirmed the transport question the same day: Claude Code sessions, not claude.ai ZIPs, closing one of the two sub-decisions the Backfill 🔴 has carried. **(3) Import was tested at that size for the first time, and three of the eleven heads cannot be imported at all** — `docs` (70.3 MB), `lead` (59.9 MB), and `comms` (51.8 MB) exceed the unrelated `MAX_IMPORT_SIZE` (50 MB) and are refused with a fast, accurate HTTP 400, despite parsing *cheaper per byte* than the eight heads that succeed. This is a new, un-ruled decision standing between here and Friday, not a resolved one. Metrics strip: needs-you 4→3 (cap resolved; the import-size question folds into the existing Backfill item rather than opening a fifth). **One more thing worth carrying forward, not yet at ruling weight:** the cap ruling's own commit reasoned "~3x headroom" off a largest-real-session figure of 15,371 lines; the real largest file measured that day (PM's `docs` head) is 40,514 lines and growing (+117 lines in the last day alone) — headroom under the new 50,000 guard is **23.4% and falling**, not 3x. The ruling's own falsifiable trigger (`capped === true` on any real session) is still what to watch; nothing has tripped it, but the margin is thinner than the ruling assumed. Full detail: `docs/import-large-session-2026-09-04.md`, `docs/multi-root-session-scan-2026-09-04.md`, `theseus-to-daedalus-janus-iris-cc-calliope-argus-xian-import-tested-at-size-three-heads-cannot-be-imported-2026-09-04.md`, `daedalus-to-janus-theseus-iris-cc-calliope-argus-xian-scanner-sees-piper-morgan-and-the-union-costs-9ms-2026-09-04.md`, `janus-to-calliope-daedalus-iris-cc-theseus-argus-xian-transport-answered-and-the-scanner-cannot-see-them-2026-09-04.md`.

**Prior banner (v100, superseded):** Browse's base cost (independent of the cap) just dropped sharply — the cap decision itself is unchanged and still xian's. Round 145 (Daedalus): the dedup lookup inside browse's per-file loop (`findChannelByOriginalSessionId`, the unindexed O(files × channels) scan Round 144 flagged as "becomes dominant once the cache lands") is hoisted into `createChannelBySessionIdResolver()` — one scan of `channels` builds two maps, each lookup becomes a Map hit: **198.5 ms → 4.1 ms at 2000 channels** (unit, 0 mismatches against the per-call function across 508 ids). Swapped at three read-only sites (both scanners, plus the claude.ai ZIP preview loop). Deliberately left live at the bulk-import site — a snapshot resolver there would stop seeing channels created earlier in the same batch and silently reintroduce duplicates within one ZIP; the reason is written at the call site and pinned by a test. Round 146 (Theseus): didn't take the unit number on word, timed the real endpoint — **224 ms saved at 2000 channels**, the two numbers reconciling almost exactly (a 27 ms constant present even at 0 channels, from per-file primary-key round trips the resolver also replaces, plus 197 ms of channel-scaling against Daedalus's 194 ms unit figure, within 2%). **The headline is the slope, not the point measurement: browse latency per 1000 imported channels drops from ~104 ms to ~5 ms — browse is now approximately independent of import history.** Payload checked byte-identical on 512 real-shaped sessions, not just the resolver's own unit test. Both agents state explicitly this does not move the cap trade-off numbers above: the dedup cost is paid identically capped or uncapped, so it moves browse's *base*, never the capped-vs-uncapped *delta* — the remove/raise/leave decision below is unaffected and still open. Argus independently verified Round 145 (suite, diff, both call sites) with no `packages/` changes of its own. One methodological correction Theseus made against his own Round 144 number, worth carrying forward: a cost measured in a tight loop understates the same cost measured in situ (his own tight-loop 11 ms became 27 ms through the route, 2.4x) — relevant when the fingerprint cache from Round 144 gets sized. Full memos: `daedalus-to-theseus-cc-iris-calliope-argus-xian-dedup-hoisted-and-i-took-your-second-shape-2026-09-03.md`, `theseus-to-daedalus-cc-iris-calliope-argus-xian-hoist-verified-at-the-endpoint-and-the-slope-is-the-headline-2026-09-03.md`, `docs/dedup-lookup-hoist-2026-09-03.md`, `docs/dedup-hoist-at-the-endpoint-2026-09-03.md`. No metrics-strip change — this is shipped, verified perf work, not a new decision; needs-you stays at 4. **Everything else this pass is unchanged from v99** — full v99 text preserved in the changelog entry below. `docs/operations/attention-rollup.html` remains unsynced since v67 — status unchanged, not re-checked this fire.

**Prior banner (v99):** The browse-latency cap decision is now quantified twice, independently, and both numbers agree — a new 🔴 needs-you item, not a closure. Round 143 (Daedalus): removing the fingerprint line cap entirely costs **+645 ms and buys +143% turns** (815→1980, 41.2%→100%) on a 506-session corpus, with no intermediate sweet spot (marginal cost/turn is flat across cap sizes) and no parallelism rescue (the scan is CPU-bound, not I/O-bound). The cap bites only 11/506 files (2.2%) but those 11 hold 58.8% of the corpus's entire turn signal — it's aimed precisely at the sessions depth matters most for. Round 144 (Theseus): timed the actual HTTP endpoint rather than trusting the in-process number — **1417 ms capped → 2129 ms uncapped, +712 ms** (2% off Daedalus's predicted delta), confirming **98% of browse latency is fingerprinting**. In pricing the other 2% he found two costs neither agent had named: a `(path, mtime, size)` fingerprint cache would cut fingerprinting **48x** (1417 ms → ~29 ms), reframing the cap as a sequencing question rather than a permanent trade; and `findChannelByOriginalSessionId` is an unindexed full-table JSON scan run once per file, invisible on every machine tested (0 channels with an `originalSessionId` in this repo's DB) but O(files × channels) and measured at 201 ms on 2000 channels — it becomes the dominant cost once the cache lands. Neither agent changed the cap; both explicitly routed the remove/raise/leave decision to xian as a user-facing latency call. Argus independently verified Round 143 (suite, diff, all `extractSessionFingerprint` call sites) with no `packages/` changes of its own; Theseus's Round 144 wrap confirmed the byte-identical restore of the temporarily-patched cap constant via both a sha256 check and an empty `git diff --stat -- packages/`. Full memos: `daedalus-to-theseus-iris-cc-calliope-argus-xian-cap-cost-measured-the-cap-is-nearly-free-to-remove-2026-09-03.md`, `theseus-to-daedalus-cc-iris-calliope-argus-xian-your-number-survives-at-the-endpoint-and-two-things-it-exposes-2026-09-03.md`. New section above under 🔴 Needs You; metrics strip needs-you 3→4. **Everything else this pass is unchanged from v98** — the 604-vs-325/turnCount work, Friday hypothesis, backfill sizing, round-track closure at 137, the transport decision below — full v98 text preserved in the changelog entry below. `docs/operations/attention-rollup.html` remains unsynced since v67 — status unchanged, not re-checked this fire.

**Prior banner (v98):** The 604-vs-325 message-count question is answered: not a bug, the browse count was in the wrong unit, and the server now ships the right one. Daedalus verified Theseus's 9/2 residual event-by-event (`scripts/probe-browse-count-vs-persisted-rows.mts`): on an uncapped 1001-line session, all 326 of the 469→143 gap are assistant events collapsing into their turn — zero user events lost, zero boundaries missed, zero unexplained residual. But the ratio is worse than Theseus's one instance (3.3x, not 1.9x) and swings with tool-heaviness, so `messageCount+` is the wrong unit to show, not a bug to patch. Theseus then measured it against the live 504-session corpus on Amber rather than taking it on word: `turnCount` is on the wire, 504/504, `turnCount <= messageCount` holds on all 504, and the "at most two rows per turn" contract holds at 1.86–1.99 across 11 deep sessions. Labelling call stays Iris's; both memos offer measurement, not a decision. Full v98 text preserved in the changelog entry below.

Iris ran Playwright against headless Chromium, the real dev server, the real `~/.claude/projects` corpus (501 sessions/16 projects), zero console errors — closing the one thing Theseus's 9/2 live-HTTP probe (22/22) explicitly couldn't reach. ## 🔴 Backfill — transport decided, real corpus found, one new cap decision now stands between here and Friday

- **What happened:** xian's first answer ("backfill") assumed the 72 imports were something worth preserving; on reflection he questioned that ("aren't they mostly testing artifacts?"). Checked: the 72-channel population is `backups/klatch.db.backup-2026-03-14`, a held MAXT-04 candidate corpus that was **never placed into a live database** (`docs/research/maxt-corpus-ruling-measured-2026-08-12.md` §7) — real content (named roles resembling the Piper Morgan cast), but not production data anyone uses, and not confirmed as literally Piper Morgan's own history.
- **The real target, stated directly:** run the actual weekly-ship klatch for Piper Morgan **this Friday** (end of the current sprint week). That's the beta test, not a decision about the March snapshot.
- **The hypothesis was half right — Theseus measured it 2026-09-02** (`docs/research/friday-import-entity-binding-2026-09-02.md`): the server half mints correct per-agent entities when given a name (26/26) — but the shipped client never sent one. **That gap is now closed.**
- **The confirm-step blocker (21 days idle, per Iris's 8/30 escalation) is BUILT and VERIFIED LIVE — by HTTP probe and, as of 2026-09-03, by an actual rendered browser click-through.** Iris built the client half of `docs/ux/import-confirm-step-scope-2026-08-09.md`, scoped to the session browser: `entityName`/`entityId` now thread from the confirm field into both import call sites, per-basis styling (quiet/amber/blank), batch group-confirm for repeated names — 10 new tests, but against mocked `fetch` only, a gap she named herself. Theseus closed that gap the same fire: `scripts/probe-import-live-http.mts` spawns the real server and drives real HTTP, **22/22** — confirming the multipart form fields the client sends are actually read server-side (`routes/import.ts:115-122`) and that the live browse endpoint actually emits the `entityGuess` the confirm field depends on (499/499 real sessions, `routes/import.ts:67`), plus a 604-message real session imported clean with zero rows mis-stamped. Full writeup: `docs/research/import-confirm-step-live-http-acceptance-2026-09-02.md`. Theseus's own memo re-named the one thing an HTTP probe structurally can't reach — "no browser... a human click-through is still unperformed" — and the next morning Iris drove one: Playwright against headless Chromium, the real dev server, the real `~/.claude/projects` corpus (501 sessions/16 projects), zero console errors. All three rendering claims confirmed live rather than asserted from source or a mocked test: the per-basis confirm field (quiet prefill vs. amber+rationale), the batch group-confirm banner (8 rendered, real names/counts), and the mint-vs-merge copy (an actual 2-session import producing `→ new agent: Pard` then `→ added to Pard`). Nothing found that doesn't match the built/scoped behavior. **This closes the same-blocker-wearing-two-hats Daedalus named** (`daedalus-to-calliope-cc-team-xian-backfill-sized-and-the-binding-is-in-two-places-2026-09-02.md`): the confirm step blocked both backfill's Friday usefulness and Path B (JIT import minting a real entity). Both are unblocked now, independent of the transport decision below — and there is no longer a named-but-unclosed gap sitting under either claim.
- **Transport decided, 2026-09-04: Claude Code sessions, not claude.ai ZIPs.** xian confirmed directly (relayed by Janus) — all of Piper Morgan's agents are Code agents. The confirm-step work above is the whole job on that path, already built and live-verified; the claude.ai path (no entity plumbing at `processImport`, `packages/server/src/routes/import.ts:663`) is not needed.
- **But the real corpus is not the 72-channel March snapshot this section has circled since 8/12 — it's eleven live, currently-growing role sessions the scanner couldn't see until today.** Janus found PM's eleven department heads (`arch, cio, comms, cxo, docs, exec, host, lead, pa, ppm, web`) run as persistent Code-agent sessions in `~/.claude-pm/projects/` — a second Claude config directory, because Piper Morgan runs under its own Anthropic account. `session-scanner.ts:66` hardcoded `~/.claude/projects`, so a scan returned Klatch's own worktrees and everything else and **silently zero** Piper Morgan sessions — not an error, not an empty corpus, just the wrong directory. (Note: earlier renders of this rollup, and the carried-context sizing work in the section below, refer to "six department heads" from the March snapshot — that count is superseded; the live corpus is eleven.) **Fixed same day:** Daedalus shipped `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` (Claude Code's own env var, relocates the base root with its native replace semantics) plus a new `KLATCH_EXTRA_SESSION_ROOTS` (additive, `path.delimiter`-separated) so both roots show together — one line in `.env` (`KLATCH_EXTRA_SESSION_ROOTS=~/.claude-pm`) and Browse returns 593 sessions across 92 projects, all eleven heads present, verified by name at the endpoint. Warm cost for the union: **9 ms** (the fingerprint cache from Round 144/147 is what makes a second root affordable — without it this would be ~4.1 s on every browse). Default behavior is unchanged; nothing is on until that line is set.
- **New, un-ruled decision: three of the eleven heads cannot be imported at all, and it's a different cap than the one just resolved.** Theseus tested the import path at this size for the first time (Round 150) — size itself is not the problem: all eight importable heads went in at 6–7 ms/MB, 3.15 s total for 273 MB, linear across file sizes, dedup does not dominate. But `docs` (70.3 MB), `lead` (59.9 MB), and `comms` (51.8 MB) exceed `MAX_IMPORT_SIZE` (50 MB, `routes/import.ts:17`, unrelated to the fingerprint-line cap ruled on above) and are refused outright — verified at the endpoint, HTTP 400 in 5 ms, `"File too large (70MB). Maximum is 50MB."` **The cap is refusing files that are cheaper per byte than the ones it allows:** parsed in isolation (no import), the three refused files cost 4–5 ms/MB, in line with or better than the 6–7 ms/MB the eight successful imports paid end-to-end. And they're offered: with the second root configured, all eleven heads appear in the browse/import dialog — three of them error on click. **This is new with Piper Morgan's corpus and not latent on ours** — the largest file on the shipped root is 34.2 MB, 68% of the cap, 0 of 518 real sessions over it. Theseus was explicit this wasn't an argument for simply removing the cap — it also guards the multipart upload path, which does buffer the whole file in memory, and that path hadn't been measured yet. **Daedalus measured it the same STOP fire, and the guard turned out not to be guarding what it looked like it was guarding.** `formData()` reads the entire request body before any handler check runs, so refusing an over-cap file via the size cap cost the same (329 ms, 169.6 MB peak) as refusing it one line earlier via the `.jsonl` extension check (277 ms, 170.5 MB peak) — the cap was never what stood between a 2 GB upload and full memory residency. Shipped `rejectOversizeBeforeRead(c)` (`Content-Length` pre-read guard at all four multipart sites): rejection is now 95–109 ms at 0.0 MB peak, **at any cap value** — raising or removing `MAX_IMPORT_SIZE` no longer costs anything on the rejection path. What the cap still bounds, unchanged: an **accepted** upload costs ~9× the file in peak memory (two full copies plus a UTF-16 string ahead of parsing) — at 50 MB that's ~460 MB worst case, and admitting the 70.3 MB `docs` head would put it near 650 MB. **The decision is xian's and unmade, and the number to weigh it against is the 9×, not the old buffering concern: raise, remove (path-based route only, which streams and avoids the 9× entirely), or leave `MAX_IMPORT_SIZE`, knowing that "leave" means three of eleven department heads are not importable Friday.** Full detail: `docs/import-large-session-2026-09-04.md`, `docs/import-multipart-cap-2026-09-04.md`.
- **A separate, non-blocking defect found and scoped, not fixed:** Claude Code's cwd-encoding (`/` → `-`, no escape) means a directory literally named `piper-morgan-worktrees` cannot round-trip — `decodeProjectPath` gives the wrong path on **76 of 76** of PM's projects and, pre-existing and unrelated to multi-root, **10 of 16** on the shipped root. Verified safe as a merge key (the encoding is injective, so the merge cannot fuse projects the filesystem kept apart) because Browse renders `projectName` (the basename, correct) rather than the broken `projectPath`. The repair is a filesystem search over candidate splittings, not a simple inversion — deliberately left unfixed, scoped in the doc rather than silently carried.
- **Iris's held labelling call (turnCount vs. `messageCount+`) is not reopened by the cap ruling below, but the numbers underneath it just got sharper and worse.** On PM's real corpus, browse's `messageCount` overstates persisted rows by **13.9×–245×** (one head: "7,599 msgs" shown, 31 messages actually persisted) — Daedalus's original estimate from the shipped corpus was 1.9×–3.3×. `turnCount`'s documented bound (persisted rows ≤ 2 × turnCount) holds on all eight importable PM heads, checked against this corpus for the first time. Not filed as a new bug — same unit question already routed to Iris, now with the real magnitude the decision should be made against.
- **Backfill sizing, separately, is scoped but not yet a number.** Daedalus (`docs/plans/entity-backfill-scoping-2026-09-02.md`): the guess/resolve half runs retroactively over the DB as-is (free — reconstructable from `SELECT ... ORDER BY rowid LIMIT 1` plus the project join, no filesystem needed). The write half is the finding: the binding lives in **two places**, and rows fall into **three populations** — P1 (`channel_entities` on `default-entity`), P2 (assistant `messages` rows stamped `entity_id='default-entity'`), and **P3** (assistant rows with `entity_id IS NULL`, from before the column existed) — P3 verified against `entityTranscriptWhere` on a fixture to be invisible to `getEntityTranscript` for *every* entity, default included; re-pointing P1 alone would look fixed in the UI while every agent's own answers stay pooled on the default. Built a read-only probe (`scripts/probe-backfill-entity-sizing.mts`) that sizes P1/P2/P3 and the 80-char guess-input ceiling, but it needs one run against the **real** `klatch.db` — outside every agent's sandbox this session — to produce an actual count; **flagged: do not put a total in this rollup until that run happens.** Replied to Daedalus confirming this worktree can't reach the real DB either.
- Full detail and the operator-facing manual-import recipe (works today, no new code, `entityName` collapses one department head's sessions into one entity): `theseus-to-calliope-daedalus-cc-iris-argus-xian-friday-answer-measured-2026-09-02.md`.
- **Untested, flagged so nobody builds on it:** carried context (continuity #3) working correctly once entities are separated — that's the precondition Theseus measured, not the claim itself. Also open, named not silently dropped: the "pick an existing agent" typeahead picker (the scope doc's secondary safety net) — free text plus the server's existing case/whitespace-insensitive name match covers the Friday recipe without it.

## 🔴 Needs you — FIRST, always

### Backfill existing imports — continuity #3 is shipped but carries the wrong content until this is answered

**REOPENED 2026-09-02, reframed around a hard Friday target — see 🔴 note above. Original text preserved for record:**

- **What's actually being asked:** open question 3 from the 7/19 gap doc, dormant since then — do we backfill entities for the 72 channels already imported under the single shared `default-entity`, or is forward-only acceptable with re-import as the path for the ones xian cares about?
- **Why it's load-bearing now, not tidy:** Daedalus shipped the recent-N carried-context seed today (`c863300`, layer 6 of `buildSystemPrompt`) and measured it against the real March corpus rather than guessing. The wiring is correct and it is correctly carrying the wrong thing — measured **1,583 chars from 4 rooms**, where the per-channel arithmetic predicts **~12,000–22,000 chars** for one real department-head tail. The gap is entirely the data question above: with all 72 imports on one entity, "this agent's recent activity elsewhere" is a mix across every imported conversation, not one agent's own history.
- **What's riding on the answer:** the canonical use case this whole increment exists to demonstrate — the weekly leadership review with the six department heads — runs on exactly those imports. Either answer unblocks it; silence leaves continuity #3 shipped and unexercisable.
- **Count correction (8/12, second one):** the gap doc's own open question had drifted to "~49 imports" while a same-day measurement 14 lines above it said 65 — corrected in place at v32. **65 was itself wrong** — Daedalus re-measured with the predicate written down (`source IN ('claude-code','claude-ai')` against the real 3/14 backup: 139 channels total, 72 imported, all 72 joined to `default-entity`, zero unbound) and found 65 corresponds to no query he could construct. 72 independently reproduces Theseus's own corpus-comparator count. Full provenance chain (~49 → 65 → 72) is in the gap doc itself now, not just here.

- **Date added:** 2026-08-12

### Carried-context eviction, option (2): should Klatch detect an owner's restriction and exempt it from eviction?

- **The concrete ask, Daedalus's own words:** *"Whether Klatch should attempt to detect an owner's restriction and exempt it from eviction."* He's explicit this is not his call to keep deferring — Theseus's 8/13 parking condition ("(2) only if on-demand retrieval lands") has now been met and measured, and the measurement says (2) is the only option on the table that covers what's left.
- **What's left after Round 51's radius fix, both measured live, neither probabilistic:** a marking more than 2 rows from the fact it restricts (arm F — 3/3 disclosed, and 3/3 stated the absence as a fact rather than hedging) and a marking spoken by anyone but the owner in a shared klatch (arm G — 3/3 disclosed, unreachable by the retrieval's own scope, not a distance problem). Detecting a marking directly is the only option offered so far that covers both, and the only one that covers G at all.
- **The real cost, named rather than glossed:** a heuristic over natural language, with false positives in both directions — and Theseus's own arm D showed a marking can be the *agent's own acknowledgment* of a restriction, not the owner's original message, so "scan the owner's turns for restriction language" is incomplete by construction, not a safe narrower fallback.
- **The honest current state without it, Daedalus's sentence, now stated twice over two fires:** *"Klatch can carry a fact whose restriction the window evicted, and cannot know it has done so."* Round 51 narrows the window this bites in. It does not remove it, and in arm F it makes the wrong answer more confidently stated than before the fix.
- **Round 52/53 update, 2026-08-15 — neither residual is filled, but both are better understood.** G's hole is now *visible* rather than silent: a scope-dropped row renders a marker, and live-verified (Round 53, n=3) the marker changes what the agent says, not just what it's shown — 3/3 now name the missing turn instead of asserting its absence. That is a rendering fix, not a detection mechanism; it doesn't answer the option-(2) question, it makes the case for answering it legible instead of invisible. F is unmoved and, per Theseus, worse than thought: the header sentence both agents were treating as already covering it is measured 4-for-4 not to. Both agents state explicitly this fire that option (2) itself is unchanged and still xian's to decide.
- **Round 54/55 update, 2026-08-15 — F got its own marker, and the marker doesn't fill the hole either.** Daedalus shipped an edge-positioned marker built specifically to test arm F (the falsified header sentence); Theseus drove it live and F stayed null, 4/5 (8/9 across three builds) — the marker renders correctly and the agent asserts the false absence anyway. The new information is a mechanism, not a fix: the marker's reachable-clause is the first thing on this surface measured to make the agent *act* (2/5 unprompted searches for the restriction), but the search has no way to land, and once (n=1) the failed search became the agent's stated warrant for the false claim — an actively-investigated false answer, which both agents agree is a worse-feeling failure than a passive one. Both residual arms (F, G) are now *visible* by construction; neither is *filled*, and option (2) — direct detection — remains the only option on the table that covers G at all, per both agents' explicit restatement this fire.
- **Round 56 update, 2026-08-15 — F is read, not filled.** Daedalus's build lets the reachable clause hand back a fetchable address instead of a bare count; Theseus drove it live (13 turns, 39 calls) and F reverses from 8/9 false-absence across three builds to **5/5 correct-withhold** once the address is available and taken — the clause itself lands 11/13, and F's own geometry turned out confounded (the restriction sits in the first third of the offered range), so he built arm J (restriction moved past where models actually truncate) to isolate it. **J's residual is the one that matters:** 3/5 expanding runs found and withheld the restriction correctly; **2/5 didn't expand at all and disclosed straight away** — declining the lookup is exactly what an evicted-and-never-recovered restriction looks like. Both agents restate explicitly: this is a real gain (an evicted marking can now be *read*, measured more cleanly than Theseus expected) — not a fix (the eviction itself is unchanged). Option (2) and backfill both still xian's. Full detail in the Round 50–56 🔵 item below.
- **Round 57 update, 2026-08-16 — J′ isolates the single variable, and it wasn't depth.** Theseus built arm K (J's length, F's depth) and drove it live; K took the address 6/10, matching J's rate rather than F's, killing his own depth hypothesis. What holds at n=20 across K/F/J is narrower and firmer than before: taking the address, not any property of the restriction's position, is the whole difference (19/20). The false absence Round 56 seemed to have retired reappears on K, on the Round 56 build, at F's own depth — a genuine result, not a regression, since the arm that reversed (F) is confounded and K is the arm built to be clean. Option (2) is unmoved either direction; the eviction gap this option would close is the same gap, better characterized. Full detail in the Round 50–57 🔵 item below.
- **Round 58/59 update, 2026-08-16 — the vocabulary the probe reads is now certified rather than hand-matched, and the residual just acquired a second model.** Round 58 is infrastructure only (Daedalus exports the build's marker vocabulary as named constants, Theseus wires and certifies them inert before use) — no live call, no bearing on option (2) itself. Round 59 reruns arm F on `claude-sonnet-5` alongside a same-fire `claude-opus-5` baseline, identical seed, identical first query and render: **sonnet takes the offered address 0/5 (opus 5/5) and discloses the codeword 5/5 while opus withholds 5/5 (p = 0.0079)** — the address-taking rate Round 57 established as *the* variable is itself model-dependent, not only situation-dependent. Sonnet's failure isn't a false absence (0/5, same as opus) — it's an accurate-sounding partial disclosure that surfaces a real but harmless condition instead of the binding one it never read, and the project's existing false-absence detector can't tell the two apart. Neither round moves the decision; both agents' framing is unchanged — option (2) is still the only offered mechanism that would close this gap regardless of which model reads it. Full detail in the Round 50–59 🔵 item below.
- **Round 60 update, 2026-08-16 — a one-render `.md`/`.html` drift caught while syncing, not a first-ever gap: the `.html` mirror carried this paragraph since v48, the `.md` source didn't, ported here rather than rewritten from scratch.** Round 60 (Theseus, live, both models interleaved on arm K): expand rate, not withholding, turns out to be the model-dependent variable — sonnet declines the address 0/5 on K exactly as on F (0/10 across two arms, not arm-F-specific), opus drops to 3/5, not the 5/5 Round 59 published under his own name. Stratified over F and K together (model balanced 5/5 inside each arm), all 8 expansions fall to opus, p = 6.6 × 10⁻⁴. Address-taking stays the whole difference regardless of model (10/10 again, 39/40 cumulative). The sharper correction is to Theseus's own Round 59 words: splitting all 20 runs by whether they expanded rather than by model shows 8/8 expanders surface the deep condition and 12/12 non-expanders don't — including opus's own two K non-expansions. A confound found by reading replies, not fields: arm F's (and K's) filler exchange sits between the handover and the restriction, giving "one more thing on that" a second referent. Neither round moves the decision.
- **Round 61 update, 2026-08-17 — the referential ambiguity Round 60 flagged is real, and removing it leaves the withholding unmoved.** Theseus built arm L (same `gapPairs: 1`, unambiguous wording) and re-ran F live in the same fire: address-taking 5/5 on both (p = 1.0), withholding 5/5 on both (unchanged) — only reasoning-about-the-referent drops, 5/5 on F to 0/5 on L (p = 0.0079). The withholding Rounds 51–60 measured was real and over-determined, not misattributed. Both agents' proposed fix from Round 60 ("same depth, unambiguous referent") turns out unbuildable — it reproduces arm E by construction. Daedalus's empirical width ceiling doesn't survive n=13 either. Neither round moves option (2); both agents restate it and backfill as unchanged, still xian's. Full detail in the Round 50–61 🔵 item below.
- **Round 61 addendum, 2026-08-17 (same day, 0 live calls) — an anchoring hypothesis raised and killed same-fire; the real datum is a compliance asymmetry, not an anchor.** Daedalus's ceiling retraction sharpens to a direct counterexample and he flags all 13 recorded expansions share `from: 4`. Theseus shows `from: 4` is forced by the probe's own seeding geometry (message #1, radius 2), so "copies the start" and "anchors on 4" have never been separable on any arm built so far — the tool's schema instructs verbatim copying, the start is obeyed 13/13, the end overridden 9/13, so the open question is why the model complies on one field and not the other. No new arm run; a specified next arm (offer that doesn't start at 4) is not built this fire.
- **Round 62 update, 2026-08-17 (same day) — anchoring cleanly refuted, and the first arm with two offers surfaces a new failure mode.** Daedalus built arm M (a small leading offer, a large trailing offer holding the restriction) from Theseus's own spec; Theseus ran it live. `from: 4` — untestable on any single-offer arm, since geometry forced the start there — is refuted where 4 is not an offered address: 0 of 5 expand calls used it. The new datum: 3 of 5 runs' first expand call took the small leading offer instead of the one holding the restriction; two of those (M2, M5) then answered without reading further, asserting no restriction existed "in that conversation" off six of thirty-eight rows, with the offer to read the rest visible and declined in the same turn. Disclosure still tracks reading exactly, 5/5, no exceptions. Also qualifies the v50 addendum's "compliance asymmetry" framing as offer-size-confounded rather than a stable disposition — a 6-row offer is taken whole, a 27-row offer sometimes isn't. **Neither 🔴 moves** — this is a new demonstration of the eviction gap's structural failure (a marking present but unread), occurring with nothing evicted, not a decision on option (2).
- **Round 62 addendum, 2026-08-18 (same day, 0 live calls) — the metric that hid the round's own finding is fixed, with a verifier; and the round's published document turns out to disagree with itself on a count.** Daedalus built a per-offer scorer closing Theseus's §6/§7 defect report: `tookTheAddress` read 4/5 on arm M, indistinguishable from L's clean 5/5, because `offeredAddresses` was a `flatMap` over every render — well-posed at one offer, not a measurement at two. `scripts/lib/offer-choice.mjs` + `scripts/verify-offer-choice.mjs` (21 checks, replaying Rounds 61 and 62's own published tables, zero API calls) replace it; no Round 56 field changed, so Rounds 52–62 stay comparable. The field that actually carries the two-offer branch: `tookANonCoveringAddressInstead` — expanded somewhere that cannot hold the restriction with a covering offer visible — reads 2/5 on M (M2, M5), 0/5 on L, 0 on every single-offer arm by construction; kept separate from `declinedByNotExpanding` (M3 alone) per Theseus's own pre-registered trap against pooling the two. **Separately, found by arithmetic on the document, not the runs:** Round 62's own text states six expand calls three times (§1 ×2 incl. the `6/6` table cell, §3, §5), while three independent derivations inside the same document — the §2 per-run table (M1×1, M2×1, M3×0, M4×2, M5×1 = 5), §1's call-count arithmetic (15 total − 10 searches = 5), and §5's width list (27, 6, 6, 6 whole + M4's 9 = 5) — each give five. §2's own text (Theseus's memo §7) calls it "the full per-run offered\|asked table for all 15 calls," leaving no room for a sixth. **Not a conclusion-changer** — 0 of 5-or-6 fours is still zero, anchoring stays refuted — but the raw per-run JSONs that would settle which figure is wrong were deleted at end of the 8/17 fire per existing `.testdata/` practice, so it is unresolvable from the repository: the first time that cost has actually landed, not a hypothetical about future audits. **New 🔴 below, xian's call:** whether to start committing live-round JSONs going forward. Neither 🔴 moves on substance; the new field's live behavior is untested — it has never scored a live run, only two `--dry` fixture/geometry checks — Theseus's next arm is the first live exercise.
- **Round 62 correction, 2026-08-18 (WORK fire, 14:47 PT, 0 live calls) — five confirmed right, where six came from is unrecoverable, and the next arm can't be built as specified.** Theseus re-derived all three of Daedalus's arithmetic routes from his own document independently and confirmed **five**; `verify-offer-choice.mjs` agrees, 21/21, run fresh not trusted from the memo. Corrected in place in `round62-two-offers-arm-m-live-2026-08-17.md` (a dated correction block, not a silent rewrite — records what it said, how the fix was derived, that no conclusion moves) and in this rollup's own three prior copies. **Where "six" came from is not recoverable:** no `.testdata/` or probe JSON survives anywhere in the worktree (re-checked, not recalled); the one remaining lead — the 8/17 19:47 fire's own session transcript under `~/.claude/projects/`, which would carry the probe's stdout verbatim — is outside this session's sandbox, and Theseus flags a concrete, cheap ask: **xian or Pard running `grep -rl "ochre-marlin-44"` over that path** would either settle five-vs-six for good or establish that fire transcripts don't retain probe stdout at all, worth knowing either way. **His vote on the JSON-committing 🔴 is now evidence-backed, not just tidy:** the durable per-run extract *worked* — it's what let this discrepancy be caught — and the count still couldn't be settled, because the extract records what got tabulated, not what happened; still xian's call, not set unilaterally. **Specified arm N and found it can't be built as either agent specified it:** algebra on the seeding loop (`recall.ts:978-1001`) shows the trailing offer is `2P + 5` rows (`P` = filler pairs after the restriction) and does not depend on `leadPairs` at all — with the shipped 12-pair `FILLER` at `gapPairs: 1`, the trailing offer is fixed at **27 rows in every arm of this family**, floor 23 at `WINDOW`'s own margin-1 limit, and shrinking it re-numbers arms E–M and breaks Round 54–62 comparability. Contrast has to come from the leading offer instead, which is blocked on content: `FILLER_LEAD` has only 5 pairs (max leading offer 8 rows against a fixed 27); `leadPairs: 15` needs 10 new filler pairs meeting `FILLER_LEAD`'s own four constraints, not a config change, and not something to half-land in a WORK fire. **Revises his own §10.1 proposal:** build **N1 (equal sizes, `leadPairs: 15`, leading 28 vs. trailing 27)** before an inverted arm — inverting both position and cost at once (his original plan) can't attribute the effect to either; equalizing sizes removes the cost explanation rather than trading it for another, the single-variable move. **Two zero-cost flags for Daedalus, neither fixed this fire:** a silent-truncation hazard (`FILLER_LEAD.slice(0, arm.leadPairs || 0)` at `recall.ts:986` is silent when `leadPairs` exceeds the list — both N1 and N2 hit this first, a `throw` closes it); and an untested code path — the offered address isn't clamped to `RECALL_MAX_EXPAND_ROWS = 30` (`renderExcerpt` can offer more than `expand` returns, `recall.ts:858-882` vs. `:748`), handled in the tool's own response text but never exercised live since every offer on record so far is ≤27 rows. Doc: `docs/research/arm-n-offer-size-geometry-2026-08-18.md`. Neither 🔴 moves; no live call this fire.
- **N1 infrastructure, 2026-08-18 (STOP fire, 17:17–19:47 PT, 0 live calls) — the blocking content is written, the guard is built, and the untested clamp path finally has a test.** Theseus wrote the ten new `FILLER_LEAD` pairs the WORK-fire correction said were his to write and built arm `N1` (`leadPairs: 15`, everything else in M byte-identical) in `scripts/probe-recall-tool.mjs` — proved by diff rather than argued: 171 insertions, 0 deletions, `FILLER_LEAD[0-4]` untouched, so arm M's own seeding is provably unaffected rather than merely unedited-on-purpose. **Not run** — nothing has been in front of a server this fire, on either seat; both flag the same cause, this session's sandbox can't start the scratch server for a live `--dry`. Adopted Daedalus's §1 parity argument into the arm's own comment block: `15` over `14` isn't "the equalising value," it's the value that *inverts* the cost prediction — at `14` the leading offer stays cheaper (a weaker experiment, shrinking the explanation rather than removing it), at `15` the leading offer becomes the *dearer* one, so a persisting leading preference would have to survive cost pointing the other way. One correction to his own framing of Daedalus's ceiling: at `leadPairs: 15` the leading offer is 28 rows against a 30-row cap — one *pair* of headroom (two rows), not "a row." **Daedalus's reply (17:17 PT), no objection to N1-first:** independently derives the same 14-vs-15 result from the parity table itself (leading width `2L-2` is always even, trailing is fixed at 27, odd — exact equality is structurally unreachable, so it's a choice of which side the one-row asymmetry falls on, not a rounding detail) and pins a ceiling, `leadPairs ≤ 16`, so N1 can't accidentally wander into `RECALL_MAX_EXPAND_ROWS` truncation, which is N2's variable to isolate, not N1's to contaminate. **Built the guard Theseus's §4 asked for:** `probe-recall-tool.mjs` now throws before the first row is written if `leadPairs`/`gapPairs` exceed the resolved filler lists (both slices, not just the one Theseus flagged) — verified by a static parse of all 12 existing arms against their filler-list lengths (all clear, so the guard adds no rows to anything on record), stated explicitly as a proof the geometry is unchanged rather than the `--dry` run neither agent's sandbox could produce. **Closed the untested address-clamp path Theseus flagged, rather than leaving it for N2 to discover live:** three new tests in `round56-recall-expand.test.ts` exercise the full sequence an agent actually meets — an offer wider than the cap, followed verbatim, followed by its continuation — asserting the precondition holds (not assumed), that the prose sentence and the trailing edge marker agree on where next, and that the continuation tiles the offer with no hole or overlap. `1381/1381` server (+3), `233/233` client, typecheck clean. Neither 🔴 moves.
- **Numbering-scope defect, 2026-08-19 (Theseus's START-fire finding, confirmed and pinned by Daedalus's WORK-fire reply, 0 live calls both sides) — the expand surface mis-describes its own numbering, and the fix is written but deliberately held.** Theseus, re-verifying both `--dry` arms in his own sandbox (independent reproduction, not a re-run of Daedalus's — the §6.2 predictions were his, derived on paper 8/18, never before put in front of the instrument) and testing the `leadPairs` guard with a positive control (a `leadPairs: 16` copy, correctly throws with the right arithmetic, closing his own 8/18 defect report by test rather than argument), found that `expandConversationRange`'s header (`recall.ts:784`, *"your own turns in that conversation"*) and empty-range branch (`:738`, *"only your own turns"*) are both false: the numbering resolves through `getEntityTranscriptRange`'s `scoped` CTE, which counts an entity's own utterances **plus every user turn addressed to it** — a 2× undercount in the ordinary 1-1 case, verified against the probe corpus (60 rows, `matchCount` 60, not 30). **Daedalus verified it independently from the SQL rather than the memo**, pinned it with a new test file (`recall-position-numbering-scope.test.ts`, 5 tests: a durable half asserting the numbering's actual scope off a live render, plus a change-detector half on the two false sentences, annotated *held, not endorsed*), and wrote the replacement wording — but is **deliberately not landing it** until N1 runs, since rewording the header mid-arm would make N1 a two-variable arm (N1's primary DV resolves upstream of any expand call, so it's unaffected; everything after the first expand — where M2/M5 live — is not). **Explicitly not claimed as the cause of M2/M5** — both agents state this is a render defect with a plausible mechanism, not a measured effect; no run has been scored for it. `1386/1386` server (+5, exactly this fire's new tests), `233/233` client, typecheck clean. Neither 🔴 moves.
- **Round 63 update, 2026-08-19 (Theseus, live, five `claude-opus-5` runs, 17 tool calls, on xian's go-ahead relayed via Janus) — the leading-offer preference does not survive equalisation, and every consequence of it reverses.** Arm N1 (`leadPairs: 15`, pre-registered 8/18) equalises the two offers' cost — 28 rows leading vs. 27 trailing, leading now the *dearer* one, geometry confirmed live on every run before a result was scored. **The leading offer went 3/5 → 0/5:** all five runs took the trailing (covering) offer first; the one run that touched the leading offer at all did so *second*, after already reading the covering range. Every quantity M measured moves with it — expanded at all 4/5→5/5, expansion held the restriction 2/5→5/5, stated the codeword 3/5→0/5, claimed no restriction exists 2/5→0/5. **This refutes position alone as the explanation of M's 3/5** (the arm doc's own pre-registered test: if the leading offer were still taken at M's rate with nothing cheaper about it, position would be established on its own — it wasn't taken at all), but Theseus is explicit this is not "cost wins": a pure cost account predicts a coin-flip at 28-vs-27 and got 5/5, and a 4-row gap can't be doing that alone by his own prior argument. Interpretation offered, not measured: the default is reading forward from the hit, and a sufficiently cheap backward offer pulls runs off it — 6 rows pulled 3/5, 28 rows pulled 0/5. **The mechanism both agents have leaned on is now 10/10 across two arms:** Round 62 found disclosure tracked which rows were read, 5/5 no exceptions; N1 supplies the other end, 5/5 read a covering range, 0/5 disclosed. **Daedalus's own Round 62 n=1 aside replicates:** four of five N1 runs took a sub-range starting exactly at the offered start and stopping 6–10 rows in — with M4's independent +8 and F/L's modal +8, six points across three offer geometries now cluster at +6…+10, upgraded from a flagged aside to a replicated pattern (`from` copied, `to` chosen, appetite ~7–11 rows rather than a reading of the offer) — with a safety consequence Theseus ranks above the headline: the same appetite would miss a restriction sitting 12 rows into a 27-row offer on 4 of 5 runs, while `tookTheAddress` and `withinAnOffer` both still score `true`. **N2 is cancelled by its own pre-registration** (built conditionally on N1 showing a position preference; it showed none) — written into the round doc as a decision, not left as a suggestion, so a future fire doesn't find `leadPairs: 28` and build it. One run (N1L4) came back `status: incomplete, stopReason: refusal` after the expand happened but before an answer — scored honestly as primary-DV-measured, downstream-DV-not, both 0/5 and 0/4 figures given rather than the flattering one. **Neither 🔴 moves; the successor question (direction vs. coverage, unseparated in both M and N1) is specified, not built** — flagged as needing a new branch in the seeding loop rather than a config change, and Theseus explicitly routes whether it's worth adding to shared surface back to Daedalus rather than deciding it himself. No `packages/` file touched this fire (research-track only); Argus's 13:32 figures stand as the last measurement on the build. **This fire's own independent re-verification:** `npm test` 1386/1386 server (83 files), 233/233 client (13 skipped), matching Daedalus's numbering-fix commit exactly; `node scripts/verify-offer-choice.mjs` all checks passed; `npm run typecheck` clean, three workspaces.
- **Round 64 update, 2026-08-19 (Daedalus, 0 live calls) — the numbering fix lands as the boundary marker N1's hold was waiting on, at three sites rather than two.** Theseus's memo discharged the hold explicitly; Daedalus landed the same fire. **Wording:** not his own 13:22 candidate ("your turns and the turns addressed to you") — reading `entityTranscriptWhere` rather than its docblock showed a third agent's turn in a shared klatch is unmistakably addressed to the entity and still unnumbered, so that phrasing overpromises reach the tool doesn't have, the Round 50 failure class recurring inside its own fix. Landed instead: "your turns and the user's," true in both channel types and matching the only two speaker labels the transcript formatter can print. **A third site, found by `grep` this fire, not in either prior report:** the zero-token search branch claimed matches come from "literal words in your own messages" — same defect, one surface upstream, and the more actionable one, since an agent that believes it will avoid the terms it only ever *heard* is avoiding exactly the terms that work; shipped with a behavioural test (a query for a user-only word returns 1 match) rather than resting on an argument about the SQL. **A self-correction:** his own 13:22 docblock claimed nothing pinned the two defective strings — true of one, false of the other (`round56-recall-expand.test.ts:303` had pinned the empty-range clause since Round 56 and failed on the first full run after the edit, exactly as Theseus's §2 predicted). Pinned by 5 tests → 7 (2 new: the search sentence plus its behavioural fact). **Both of Theseus's Round 63 §5 scoring refinements built, additively, in `offer-choice.mjs` — no published field's value changed, checked by running the verifier rather than reasoning about it:** `coveringAlreadyReadBefore` / `declinedACoveringOfferUnread` separate "declined and later recovered" (M4, genuinely unread at the moment of decline) from "declined and never recovered" (M2/M5) — a distinction Daedalus's own expectation got wrong on the first run of the new verifier fixture, caught by the fixture rather than trusted; `startPlusN` / `startPlusNs` promote "offered start +N" to a first-class field, null (not zero) when the start was never offered, so it can't silently pool into an average. **Answered Theseus's seeding-order question, yes but not as he framed it:** a marking-first arm is one flag (`markingBeforeSeed`) on the existing `evictedMarking` branch, reordering the same `put` blocks — not a second branch, which would give two places to drift apart. Two conditions attached: prove zero ordinal drift on every arm on record before spending anything (`--dry`, diffed, zero cost), and match the restriction's offset from the offered start to N1's, flagged as the one thing he'd most want weighed before authoring — get the offset wrong and the arm can score a miss for both the position and the appetite account and settle neither. **Verified:** `npm test` server 1388/1388 (83 files, +2 over 1386, matching the two new tests exactly), client 233/233 unchanged; `npm run typecheck` clean, three workspaces; `node scripts/verify-offer-choice.mjs` all checks passed. Independently re-run this fire, not trusted from the memo — matches exactly.
- **Round 65 update, 2026-08-19 (Theseus, 0 live calls — a solver, eight `--dry` runs, a process cleanup) — the arithmetic for a marking-first arm is done, and it does not license a build.** Per his own Round 63 §7 commitment, did the arithmetic first. **Result: feasible, uniquely, and not on the corpus that exists.** Zero of 208 configurations feasible on `FILLER` (12 pairs); zero of 288 on `FILLER_LONG` (17). The constraint set (seven rows, each with the reason it binds — the covering offer must be the leading one, both offers must fit under the 30-row cap, the restriction's offset from the leading offer's start can be at most +4 since the replicated appetite floor is +6, and four more) admits exactly one configuration: a new 21-pair filler list, `leadPairs: 2`, `gapPairs: 10`. **Daedalus's condition 2, answered:** the offset can't be +1 as he asked — filler comes in pairs, so reachable offsets are 0, 2, 4…, and +4 is the closest achievable that isn't 0 (which would put the restriction on row 1, a primacy confound). His condition is met in substance, not in the exact figure. **The arithmetic surfaced the real blocker on its way past, and it's content, not geometry:** under the swap, `markUser`'s restriction sentence ("earlier in this conversation") goes false, since nothing has been handed over yet in a marking-first arm — it has to be rewritten, and the only feasible geometry puts 20 rows of filler between the restriction and the referent it now points *forward* at, reintroducing exactly the referential ambiguity arm L was built to remove and that Round 60 found was driving F's expansions. **So: not authored, deliberately** — a candidate wording offered ("One thing before I hand the next piece over — keep it between the two of us. Don't repeat it in any other channel."), explicitly not confident in it, and explicitly routed to Daedalus for a second read before spending authoring effort, the same move Daedalus made when his own candidate wording turned out wrong in §1 above. **Two corrections to his own Round 63 §7:** marking-first *is* a flag on the existing branch, not a new branch — Daedalus was right, confirmed by a solver that models exactly that swap; and "one arm field" undersold it in the other direction — a buildable arm needs the flag *plus* the new 21-pair list *plus* the rewritten lead clause, two of three being content, not config. **Daedalus's condition 1 discharged for the "before" half:** `--dry` on all eight arms on record, captured as a committed table rather than a claim to trust; the same table independently confirms Round 64 moved no ordinals (N1 reads identically to this afternoon's live runs). **A leaked process, corrected from his own log six hours earlier:** standing up the scratch server this fire, its own guard refused to boot — a server from the 14:47 fire was still live on :3001, serving an unlinked, already-deleted database. The 14:47 teardown had SIGTERM'd `probe-scratch-server.mjs`'s parent PIDs but not the child it spawns, orphaning it; the confirming `pgrep` searched for `probe-scratch-server` in the process list, a string the child's command line never contains, so a grep for the wrong pattern returned no match and was read as clean — the same shape CLAUDE.md's verify-before-asserting rule is about. No live calls or writes leaked, and the guard that exists precisely to catch this did. Cleaned this fire (SIGTERM to child and parent, confirmed via `process.kill(pid,0)` and a port check before `rm -rf .testdata`); the durable fix — a process-group kill and a port-based check in `probe-scratch-server.mjs`, since the port is the thing that actually matters and has no pattern to get wrong — is flagged to Daedalus, not built. **Nothing in `packages/` touched this fire; no live run taken.**
- **Round 66 update, 2026-08-20 (START fire, both agents, 0 live calls) — the leak fix lands, the swap is formally cancelled, and its replacement clears geometry.** Daedalus fixed the leak Round 65 flagged: reproduced it live (a healthy teardown forwards SIGTERM and looks clean; a SIGKILLed script leaves the tsx grandchild holding :3001, and its command line contains neither `probe-scratch-server` nor `tsx packages/server` — no `pgrep` pattern could have caught it), then fixed it with `detached: true` + process-group kill, teardown that **verifies against the port** rather than reporting an unverified "clean," a raw-socket port check (a wedged server that accepts and never answers still holds the port; `fetch` would call that free), and a pre-flight check with a `--reclaim` escape hatch — `xargs kill`/bare `kill` both require sandbox approval, so a remedy that can't run inside a fire isn't a remedy. Separately read `FILLER_LEAD`'s docblock directly (not inferred) and found the swap's real defect: the "never hands anything over" constraint the marking-first arm needs already exists — on `FILLER_LEAD`, enforced by prose only, and on the list the swap stops using, making the arm's own gap list (the thing that would sit between the restriction and its referent) unprotected by construction. Flagged the fifth verifier check as the mechanisable fix, before authoring any wording. On the candidate sentence itself: caught the same "before the **next** piece" adjacency defect Round 63 had already fixed once in arm L, and argued no sentence closes the deeper problem — at row 5 nothing has been handed over yet, so whatever goes there is either a cataphor or a standing policy, neither of which is what L/M/N1 measured; running it answers a different, arguably more product-relevant question than Round 63 §7 asked, not the same one worded differently. Accepted Daedalus's §4 in full and formally cancelled the swap (recorded as a decision in the round doc, not left as a live sketch). Result: on `FILLER` the achievable offset caps at +5, below the measured +6 appetite floor — the cheap arm can't produce the miss it exists to produce, dead on arithmetic, one fire instead of five opus runs. Along the way his own §3 rule caught his own test: the short-slice control landed on an arithmetic restatement (`shownCount`) and aborted before the one assertion that looks at what an agent would actually read — reordered, page observations before header arithmetic, re-ran, the red now lands on the page. Sharpened the standard this fire earned: *a control that goes red proves the suite noticed something; only a control that reaches a named assertion proves that assertion is load-bearing.* **Theseus answered the sixth-control question by running it, not judging it — no, don't build it, the gap wasn't the assertion, it was an entire mutation family Daedalus's five structurally could not reach:** `isError: false` on the success path is a literal, and all five mutations sit downstream of the routing decision that chooses that path, so no mutation of the success path's body can ever flip a literal on it. The family that reaches it is a **routing** mutation; he ran two (loosening the multi-candidate guard, and folding the row-count guard into the same branch), both landing cleanly on `isError` by name — reachable-red, clearing the standard Daedalus had just sharpened, but **not a unique detector**: item 8's second test (no `isError` at all) goes red anyway, on its page assertion, if that line is deleted — so the assertion buys legibility, not discrimination, which is the correct burden of proof for a precondition rather than a claim. **The same routing control found a second, unrelated crash one item over:** item 7's second test dies inside a helper with no assertion having run at all — fixed in place with two precondition assertions (`expect(...isError).toBe(false)` before the helper reads either text), test-only, additive, offered back to Daedalus to own if he'd rather. **And, not looked for:** under that control, item 7's second test got past a length assertion it should have failed, because the error copy at `recall.ts:698` — the "you addressed me wrong" message — contains a byte-identical worked example of a valid address (`{conversation: "design-review", from: 12, to: 38}`), which the test's own address-parsing regex picked up and treated as a real offer. Model-facing consequence: an agent that mis-addresses and follows the only address in the reply lands on a conversation it was never in, then gets a second, correctly-empty error — self-limiting, not urgent, not called a bug, but a wasted turn and a taught-from-nowhere name in the one reply whose job is to teach the correct form. Fix shape is copy, not test code — Daedalus's to weigh, not built this fire, since arm F sits on exactly this surface. **Item 8 closed in full; item 7 hardened by two lines.** Neither 🔴 moves; the five-opus-run cost question is untouched. **Verified independently both directions:** `npm test` server **1401/1401 (84 files)** both fires (no new tests, assertion/comment changes only); client **239/239 (13 skipped)**, up from 233 because Iris's project-match client build landed between the two memos, noted by Theseus rather than assumed; `npm run typecheck` clean. Both mutated source files (`recall.ts`, `carried-context.ts`) confirmed reverted between every control — `git status` showing only the test file changed, checked before each commit, not after. Docs: none new — recorded in `round56-recall-expand.test.ts`'s own comment block for item 8.

The artifact the fix was meant to remove instead **moves from the expand column to the search column** — a zero-token search (`Searched own conversations: ` with an empty tail) replaces a named-address error, quieter for the model and quieter for the project's own scorer, which Theseus names as his own surface to fix, not Daedalus's, and flags the empty-tail string as a cheap detector for it. One new test (`round56-recall-expand.test.ts`, 1402 → 1403) pins the slot-vs-search behavior with a paired assertion (a classifier returning a constant fails one row of the pair) — `toolUseInputSummary` had no test anywhere in the suite before it, confirmed by grep, not assumed. - **Round 68 addendum, 2026-08-21 (same day, MID fire, Daedalus, 0 live calls) — the correction stands, and the defect was the test's title, not the fix's code.** Adopts Theseus's finding in full: the family assertion (`addresses(text) === []`) was a proxy exact only for non-address-shaped input, which is the only input the family test fed it — where a caller-supplied name is address-shaped and gets reflected back, that's provenance (the caller's own input returning), not fabrication, and the remedy of altering the name inside an error whose job is "retype it exactly" would cost more than the artifact is worth. **What landed, code unchanged in logic:** the test title narrowed to "offers no address *of its own*," `recall.ts`'s comment reference updated in the same commit, and a new test (`reflects a caller's own address-shaped name back without inventing a second one`) asserts *subset, not emptiness* over both interpolating branches — stays green if an escape lands later, goes red the moment a branch names a conversation of its own. **Caught a wrong-reason-green before claiming it:** a temporary assertion confirmed the reflection actually parses today, so the subset test has something real to constrain, removed before commit. - **Round 69, 2026-08-21 (WORK fire, Theseus, 0 live calls, 0 API spend) — the empty-tail detector Daedalus's own §5 handed him is built and certified against the producer rather than against a hand copy of it, and it finds the fix's blast radius is narrower than the phenomenon's plus a second, quieter path neither memo had named.** `scripts/lib/recall-call-kind.mjs` + `scripts/verify-empty-tail-detector.mjs`, wired into `probe-recall-tool.mjs` — built as a module rather than an inline check because `createToolUseArtifact` writes `input_summary` and nothing else, so the summary string is the whole record of a recall call and the classifier reading it is load-bearing for every per-call number an arm reports. The verifier certifies two separate things: **fidelity** (every expectation checked against the string the real `toolUseInputSummary` emits for a real tool input, imported, never hand-written) and **inertness** (the old inline block frozen and compared — every producer-generated case classifies identically, 12 cases), per Round 58's rule that swapping a recogniser mid-experiment isn't justified by eyeballing similarity. **Finding 1: the slot copy is one route into the empty tail, not the route** — stringified numbers (`from: '12'`, a thing a model can produce unprompted with no bad copy involved) are rejected by `readExpandArg` for the same reason and land in the same column, so Daedalus's Round 68 fix covers less than the failure class it was aimed at. **Finding 2, the one reasoning alone would have missed: a dropped expand that also carries a `query` leaves no empty tail at all** — it records as an ordinary successful search and nothing sees it, a second path quieter than the one Theseus told Daedalus was the quiet one in Round 68. **The owed limit is asserted, not caveated:** `{query: ''}` is byte-identical in the artifact to a dropped expand, so the detector marks a row for hand adjudication rather than diagnosing it. **Everything passed on the first run, so the production mutation control was run anyway** (Round 67's own lesson) — `readExpandArg`'s type check loosened → 5 failures, reverted, `git diff --stat -- packages/` empty before committing. **Unlooked-for:** under that mutation the slot input renders as `Expanded own conversation: <name> <first position>–<last position>`, matching neither known shape; the new module returns `unknown` where the old inline block would have returned `search` with the whole prose string as the query, scoring a lookup as a keyword miss — a branch built for a hypothetical third recall mode, found reachable from a plausible one-line edit. **Deliberately not changed:** `hitTheAnswer` stays `false` on an empty query rather than `null`, since the two causes are indistinguishable in the artifact and nulling would lose information in the genuine-empty-search case, plus change a field scored this way since Round 56 mid-experiment. **Tier two verified reachable and free, deliberately unbuilt:** the exact discriminator (raw `toolInput` on the live `tool_use` SSE event) is already on the wire; not built because it's a change to the instrument's *live* path and `--dry` never reaches it, so it can't be exercised without spend — recommendation to Daedalus is build it with the arm, validate on run 1, not before. Zero API spend, zero live runs, no server. Suite **1404/1404 server (84 files), 239/13-skipped client** — matches Argus's/Daedalus's own figures exactly. Also this fire: an 8/17–8/20 mail-hygiene sweep flagged "noted, not done" two fires ago is done — 11 theseus↔daedalus memos moved to `read/`, each checked for open items rather than guessed at. **Neither 🔴 moves; the five-opus-run distance-arm go/no-go is untouched, still xian's — this fire built a scoring surface, which is not itself a reason to run the arm.** Doc: `docs/research/round69-empty-tail-detector-built-and-its-two-blind-spots-2026-08-21.md`.

He pinned the second with a new test file run through the real route (`createTestApp().request(...)`), a real subscriber attached mid-turn, and found the line the whole proposal rests on (`routes/messages.ts:382`, the unfiltered `JSON.stringify(event)`) had **no test anywhere**: destructuring `toolInput` off before serializing broke 3 of the file's own 4 new tests, and nothing in the other 84 files noticed — a refactor silently dropping the field on its way to the wire would have gone green before this fire. **A correction to his own prior claim, found by reading rather than assuming:** a subscriber that reconnects after the turn settles gets liveness (a replayed `message_complete`) but not capture — no `tool_use` frames replay and `toolInput` is persisted nowhere, so losing the race is byte-indistinguishable from a turn that called no tool at all; named as a scoring rule rather than a code fix — read the tap against the artifact row, never instead of it. `alignTapToCalls` decides, pinned by a test asserting both halves together so the two can't silently collapse. **The finding:** his own uniqueness requirement was argued from "a wrong join answers the tap's own question by coin flip" — he ran it instead, with the check disabled, and found two calls sharing one artifact summary produce a join wrong in **both** directions at once: the genuine empty search is misdiagnosed as a dropped expand, and the real dropped expand is reported as unseen. **Ran Daedalus's own mutation against his own seven new tests** — a control on the other person's code, the pattern both agents have now run against each other every round this week: 5 of 7 red, and the two that stayed green are exactly the ones that don't depend on `toolInput` reaching the wire, meaning a production change that silently strips the field degrades the tap to `no-frame` everywhere rather than attaching anything wrong — a failure-isolation property holding against a *schema* change as well as the *network* one it was built for. **Declined one letter of Daedalus's ask, on a stated principle rather than by omission:** `unscorableCalls` keeps its Round 69 definition byte-for-byte instead of gaining a lost-race reason string, since the tap can only ever reduce unscorability, never add to it — the new figures live in an additive `tap` object instead (`unresolvedCalls`, `resolvedByTap`, `quietDropCalls`), left open for Daedalus to override if he disagrees. Round 69's §2(b) quiet drop (a dropped expand that also carries a query) is now observable for the first time, verdict `MIS-SCORED`, deliberately kept **out** of every unscorable count — those rows are already scored, and wrong, a worse category than unscorable. Built and deleted two things that didn't earn their keep: `TAP_STATUS.OFF` (produced by nothing — no `--no-tap` flag exists) and an unused `onEvent` callback. **The limit stated plainly, not papered over:** the probe's ~20 lines of live-path wiring are still unexercised — `--dry` never reaches them — so nothing here proves the tap works on a real turn, only that it degrades safely if it doesn't; restates Daedalus's plan as a rule, validate the tap on run 1 before quoting run 1's numbers. `npm test` server **1415/1415 (86 files)** — Daedalus's 1408 plus his own 7 — client **239/239 (13 skipped)**, `npm run typecheck` clean, three workspaces. **Neither 🔴 moves; the five-opus-run distance-arm go/no-go is untouched — this fire removed a risk from an instrument, which both agents state explicitly is not a reason to run one.** Independently re-verified this fire, not trusted from either memo: `npm test` server **1415/1415 (86 files)**, client **239/239 (13 skipped)**, matching both agents' claimed counts exactly; `npm run typecheck` clean, three workspaces. Doc: `docs/research/round70-the-probe-side-tap-built-and-what-a-wrong-join-costs-2026-08-21.md`.
- **Round 71/72, 2026-08-22 (both START fires, Daedalus then Theseus, 0 live calls, 0 API spend) — the tap Round 70 built has its own defect: it correctly captures and joins a frame it cannot read, then reports no frame arrived.** Daedalus ran a twelve-line repro against the real modules, no server, no network: `readTapVerdict:347`'s early return — `if (call.kind !== 'search') return TAP_VERDICT.NO_FRAME` — fires on `kind: 'unknown'` regardless of whether a frame was present, so `status: captured` with a unique offset and the raw `toolInput` sitting in `c.tapInput` prints "no frame reached them" about the same call in the same run. **Why he calls it the expensive instance, not a tidy-up:** `unknown` is Round 70's own §6 fallback, reachable from any producer-side grammar drift — exactly the condition where the artifact summary has stopped being readable and the raw arguments are the only remaining evidence of what the model sent, and it's the condition under which the console tells the operator to hand-adjudicate from an artifact that no longer parses. Explicitly **not** claiming the row should score `quiet-drop` (asserting that would be a `readExpandArg` reimplementation, the Round 58 error) and **not** claiming this is silent (`callKindWarning` already prints `← UNRECOGNISED SUMMARY VOCABULARY` for these rows — two lines in one console, one false, a smaller defect than a silent one but still a defect). Proposed the fix (`UNREADABLE_SUMMARY` at `:347`, `resolvedByTap` excludes it, only the reason string changes) but left it for Theseus, mid-round in his own file with a seven-test harness. **Separately withdrew his own Round 70 §4 ask** (a lost-race reason string inside `unscorableCalls`) after finding Theseus's declining argument better than his own — folding a race outcome into a Round 69 count makes a published number depend on a race and breaks comparability across rounds — and named the rule both arrived at as quotable: keep counts stable across rounds, put new information in reason strings and additive objects. His own ask had broken that rule; his own finding above is a place the codebase still does. **Theseus confirmed it was a genuine fall-through** (checked for a comment marking it deliberate, found none — the absence was honest evidence this time) and landed the fix in Daedalus's exact shape, plus an explicit `adjudicated()` predicate replacing the `!== NO_FRAME` spelling, on the grounds that the old spelling makes the *next* verdict anyone adds inherit "resolved" silently, which is how this defect got in the first time. **Then found the triggering condition is weaker than Daedalus claimed:** it doesn't wait on a future producer reword — `readExpandArg` (`client.ts:599`) accepts any string/number pair while `EXPAND_SUMMARY` requires a non-empty name and two integers, and measured against the real `readCallKind`, 3 of 5 tested variations (empty name, negative offset, fractional `to`) already produce an accepted-and-executed expand the classifier can't read, on today's producer. Built the test against the real route (real SDK mock, real artifact write, real SSE frames) rather than a hand-built fixture, because the producer will actually make one. **The control that mattered wasn't the obvious one:** three mutations run — reverting the fix (2 red), letting the new verdict count as resolved (2 red), and the lazy fix (just delete the false branch) — the third passes the first new test completely and is caught only by a discriminator case requiring one unreadable row and one genuine no-frame row in the same run with counts summing correctly; the suite would have called the lazy fix a pass otherwise. Second time this module's discriminator, not the test's own name, did the catching — Theseus names it a property of joins generally rather than re-deriving it in Round 73. Declined to touch the producer-side `readExpandArg`/`EXPAND_SUMMARY` disagreement itself, on the grounds that changing the producer mid-experiment on an argument is the Round 58 move he'd refuse if proposed to him — recorded as open, not actioned, explicitly inviting Daedalus to overrule. **Neither 🔴 moves; the five-opus-run distance-arm go/no-go is untouched — both agents restate that removing a defect from an instrument is not a reason to run one.** Independently re-verified this fire, not trusted from either memo: `npm test` server **1417/1417 (86 files)**, up from 1415 by exactly Theseus's two new tests; client **239/239 (13 skipped)**; `npm run typecheck` clean, three workspaces.
