- **Round 73/74, 2026-08-22 (Daedalus MID fire, Theseus WORK fire, 0 live calls, 0 API spend both) — the recorded summary and the executed call are computed from different values, three producer-side fixes stay refused mid-experiment, a fourth is found and also refused, and Round 72's own fix is caught pointing the operator at the wrong cause.** Daedalus, asked by Theseus's §5 whether declining to touch the `readExpandArg`/`EXPAND_SUMMARY` disagreement was over-caution: **ruled not over-caution, and extended the refusal to two adjacent changes Theseus hadn't named** — loosening `EXPAND_SUMMARY` itself (`recall-call-kind.mjs:72`), and tightening the tool's `input_schema` from `number` to `integer` (`client.ts:588-589`). Measured, on an 8-turn conversation, why: `readExpandArg` accepts any string/number pair while `expandConversationRange` (`recall.ts:686`) normalizes (trim, floor, clamp) before executing, so the recorded `inputSummary` and what the executor did are computed from different values, and each of the three available fixes changes the model-facing surface mid-experiment in a way Round 58 exists to refuse — tightening the producer would route today's loose shapes to a zero-token search error instead of an unreadable expand, a worse product outcome; loosening the classifier retroactively reclassifies stored artifacts; narrowing the schema alters what the model is invited to emit, the behaviour under study. Recorded as a change set for a round boundary, sequencing left to xian and Theseus: (3) schema, then (1) don't-touch-`readExpandArg`, then (2) loosen `EXPAND_SUMMARY`. **Made the deferral safe rather than just asserted it:** four new characterization tests (27→31 in `round56-recall-expand.test.ts`) pin today's behaviour against the real modules; a control applying fix (1) anyway turns three of his own tests red plus **one of Theseus's Round 72 tests**, mechanically coupling any future producer-tightening to the tap fix it would break. **Second finding, found while writing that control, on the model-facing surface rather than the console:** a complete answer can be told it was truncated — `expandConversationRange`'s continuation clause (`recall.ts:793`) fires on `shownRows < all.length || lastShown < to`, and when a caller's `to` runs past a conversation's real end, every row that exists is fetched and shown, yet the second disjunct still appends a continuation address that returns nothing on the next call. Reachable without any grammar drift — a well-formed, cleanly classified `expand` — whenever the model works positions out for itself rather than using an address a result gave it, which the tool description forbids but cannot enforce. **Caught his own draft wrong before committing:** the first version of the fix comment claimed deleting the clause would restore a silent-truncation failure; a control (delete the disjunct, run both files) disproved it — exactly one test goes red, his own new one, nothing else notices, so the one-line deletion is the whole fix. Left unfixed anyway, same rule applied to his own file: item (4) in the change set, independent of the other three. Corrected his own Round 72 prose in the same fire: the empty-name row is **accepted then refused** by the address guard (`recall.ts:718-731`), not "accepted and executed" as both his and Theseus's Round 72 text claimed — the row that really is accepted and executed is `from: -1`, clamped to row 1, returning eight real rows under a summary the classifier still can't read; Round 72's fix and tests are unaffected, both rows classify `unknown`. Suite **1421/1421 server (86 files)** — his own four plus Theseus's 1417 — client **239/239 (13 skipped)**, `npm run typecheck` clean, three workspaces. **Theseus, same day, found the defect in his own six-hour-old fix:** Round 72's new console line named `tapInput` as the evidence to check and then closed with "producer-side grammar drift is the likely cause" — disproved by the other half of the same commit, which measured the `unknown` branch firing on today's producer with no reword at all. Same failure shape as the bug Round 72 fixed: a confident single-cause console line that stops the operator looking further. **Not a producer change** — it rewrites the tap's own prose in a function this arm's predecessor already touched, so none of the four refused items apply. Fix: name both causes, loose-argument-first, and hand over the discriminator rather than compute it (checking `tapInput.expand` for well-formedness would reimplement `readExpandArg` inside the tap, the Round 58 error the whole join exists to avoid). Three controls run, not argued: restoring Round 72's sentence verbatim (red), the lazy over-correction — naming only the loose-argument cause and deleting the drift clause (red, and the one worth having: it shows swapping one confident cause for another loses a real one, since grammar drift stays right once arguments check out), and naming both with drift second (red, via an ordering assertion so neither the old text nor its mirror image passes). Took Daedalus's correction on the empty-name row, verified independently in the shipped file rather than off his memo (found his own line reference was one guard off — `:713`, not `:718-731`), and updated both the Round 72 doc and the Round 71 test comment; left open, not swapped, whether `from: -1` should replace the empty-name row as the Round 71 tap-test fixture — recorded in the test comment as the same class of mid-round move as a producer change, not decided unilaterally. Independently re-ran Daedalus's §4 continuation-clause control himself rather than accept the memo's numbers: exactly one red, confirming the one-line fix and agreeing without landing it. Suite unchanged at **1421/1421** — assertions added to an existing test, not new ones. **Neither 🔴 moves; the five-opus-run distance-arm go/no-go is untouched, still xian's — third consecutive fire across two agents finding defects in instruments and prose rather than data, which both restate is still not a reason to run one.** Independently re-verified this fire, not trusted from either memo: `npm test` server **1421/1421 (86 files)**, client **239/239 (13 skipped)**; `npm run typecheck` clean, three workspaces. - **Round 75/76, 2026-08-22 (both STOP fires, Daedalus 17:17 PT then Theseus 19:47 PT, 0 live calls, 0 API spend both) — the corrected console line still names a shape that can't reach the branch, and the classifier's own comment on that branch has said the opposite of true for seven rounds.** Daedalus: Round 74's "an empty **or blank** conversation name" is one word too many — measured through the shipped modules, a whitespace-only name parses cleanly under `EXPAND_SUMMARY` (`/^Expanded own conversation:\s+(.+)\s+(\d+)–(\d+)$/` — the leading `\s+` takes some of the run, `(.+)` takes exactly one space, the trailing `\s+` takes the rest), scores `ACCEPTED_EXPAND`, and never appears in the `UNREADABLE SUMMARY` count the line is explaining, while an *empty* name is one space short of that match and does. "Empty" and "blank" read as synonyms, so deleting just "or blank" (the lazy fix) leaves the operator's wrong conclusion exactly as reachable — ran it as a control, red for the right reason, alongside a verbatim-restore control also red. Rewrote the line to name the empty string exactly and state plainly that a whitespace-only name is *not* one of the shapes that explain the row (the executor refuses it too, separately). Left the underlying gap open on the standing rule: the executor (`recall.ts:688` trims, `:713` guards) treats `''` and `'   '` identically, so narrowing `EXPAND_SUMMARY` or trimming `m[1]` would move rows between verdicts mid-experiment and retire the regex's documented byte-identity guarantee to the pre-extraction probe — parked as item (5), smallest and most clearly instrument-side of the five. Two new tests (1421→1423): one pinning the executor's blank-equals-empty behaviour, one pinning the corrected console text against both controls. **Theseus, same day, later fire:** re-measured every one of Daedalus's claims through the shipped modules before accepting them — all hold, including the exact one-space-in/one-space-out detail — then checked the one thing that would have made the whole instruction circular (whether `tapInput.expand` is the wire or derived from the summary; it's the wire, `probe-recall-tool.mjs:1682`, so the instruction is followable), then opened `scripts/lib/recall-call-kind.mjs` — one commit in its entire history, Round 69, unopened by anyone in the seven rounds since. Its comment on the exact `kind: 'unknown'` branch this whole arm is about reads *"Unreachable against today's producer — and that is the point of having it."* **False, and false since it was written:** the branch fires today on the same three shapes the console line names — an exactly-empty name, a negative position, a fractional position — no third recall mode required, the branch's own comment reasoning about. The two halves of the instrument were routing the same row to opposite files: the console (`tapWarnings`) correctly tells the operator it's a model-side loose argument to adjudicate from `tapInput`; the classifier comment tells a reader the branch can't fire against today's producer, which reads as "a nonzero count is an instrument bug" — the exact wrong-file failure the last three rounds were about, one file deeper instead of one word further in. Named as his own error: the Round 72 commit subject states the branch is reachable today, in his own words, without his having opened the file whose comment says the opposite. Fixed the comment only — names the reachability, the two mechanisms, cites the tests that pin each — no code, no test added, and said explicitly why not: `tapWarnings` returns a string so its defects have a runtime surface Rounds 74/75 could put under assertion and catch within a fire; a comment has no runtime surface, nothing can assert it, and a test that goes red on any rewording would be a brittle mechanism built to guard prose. Recorded as a class that is human-mitigated, not test-guardable, rather than papered over with something that looks like coverage. Drafted and killed a second finding with a control before filing it: a plausible future tidy of `toolUseInputSummary` (floor-and-clamp, matching what the executor already does) would silently drop two of the three named shapes out of the branch and reintroduce Round 75's defect with nothing to catch it — ran the one-line mutation instead of arguing it, exactly two tests went red, and they're the right two: Daedalus's own Round 73 pair already guards that exact edit. **Neither 🔴 moves; the five-opus-run distance-arm go/no-go is untouched — fifth and sixth consecutive fires across two agents finding defects in instruments, producers, and now a seven-round-stale comment rather than in data, still not a reason to run one.** Independently re-verified this fire, not trusted from either memo: `npm test` server **1423/1423 (86 files)**, client **239/239 (13 skipped)**; `npm run typecheck` clean, three workspaces; `recall.ts:688`/`:713` grepped directly, matching both memos exactly. - **Round 77/78, 2026-08-23 (both START fires, Daedalus 09:17 PT then Theseus 10:47 PT, 0 live calls, 0 API spend both) — the first clean round in seven turns out to be clean in the code and wrong in its own demonstration.** Daedalus independently re-verified Round 76 (the comment fix) citation by citation, in the file each names — all five hold, including that the corrected comment's "demands a non-empty name" does *not* reintroduce his own Round 75 blank-name defect (`'  '` is non-empty, it matches, the comment doesn't list it) — and re-ran Theseus's control (one-line clamp-and-floor on `client.ts:621`) independently rather than trusting the memo's numbers: exactly the same two tests red, his own floor value differing from Theseus's without moving the conclusion. **First of seven consecutive fires across the two agents to find nothing in the round before it.** Turned instead to the *reasoning* in Theseus's §4, where Theseus declined to build a mechanism guarding the fixed comment and called the class "prose has no runtime surface, nothing can assert it." **Found that premise false and dated it:** the comment's proposition — that the `unknown` branch is reachable — has been under assertion, green, since Round 72 (`e8262ef`); `round71-…test.ts:448` drives the real producer via `driveWithTap` and asserts a message nearly a verbatim negation of the wrong comment, under a docstring that says in bold "the case is reachable from today's producer." `git show e8262ef --stat` is three files, none of them the classifier — both statements sat in the tree simultaneously from R72 through R75, one of them executable, and the assertion never caught the comment because it was **true**, not because it lacked a surface. Reframes the class: not unassertability but **"a green test is silent"** — a stronger claim than the one it replaces, because it also blocks the tempting reverse inference ("claims with a runtime surface get caught") that Rounds 74/75 would otherwise seem to support; both of those were caught by a person reading a sentence, with the suite green throughout. Listed and rejected three mechanisms rather than just concluding "build nothing": assert-the-comment (Theseus's, rejected on his grounds), a citation link-checker over `scripts/lib/*.mjs` comments (not brittle on rewording, genuinely fires on citation rot — rejected anyway because it would have been *vacuous on the original Round 69 comment*, which cited no test at all; it guards the corrected state and is blind to the defective one), and unbounded grep-for-contradiction. **Offered, and explicitly declined to propose as an enforced rule,** one sharpening: the comment and the R72 assertion share the noun phrase *"unknown branch"*; `grep -rn "unknown branch" scripts/ packages/` puts both on one screen, ran it, it does — named as an observation about how this one was findable, not a discipline anyone should be measured against. **Theseus (START fire, 10:47 PT)** independently re-verified every one of Daedalus's own citations and both git facts — all hold, nothing added or subtracted — then ran the one check Daedalus's memo didn't: the grep, **against the tree as it stood when the bug was live**, not today's corrected one. `git grep -in "unknown branch" e8262ef -- scripts packages` → **one hit, the assertion alone; the wrong comment is invisible to it.** The collision Daedalus demonstrated is an artifact of his own Round 76 rewrite, which quoted the assertion's message verbatim *into* the comment (`recall-call-kind.mjs:128`) — the grep was catching a citation four days younger than the bug it was offered as a cure for, the same half-objection Daedalus had just used to kill the link-checker candidate ("vacuous on the defective state, works only on the corrected one"). **Found the phrase that actually collided in the defective tree:** `today's producer` — two hits at `e8262ef`, opposite polarity, one screen, present from the exact commit Daedalus's own claim is about and staying available through R73–R75. Generalizes why the rule underdetermines which noun to grep: the Round 72 commit subject contains *both* candidate phrases in one sentence ("the unknown branch is reachable from today's producer"), and only one resolves the contradiction — identifiers are the phrase a comment sitting on the code is *least* likely to say, since the code already supplies them, while the proposition's object has to be named by anything asserting or denying it. Sharpened form: **grep the terms of the proposition, not the name of the code it's about** — falsifiable, this episode is one positive case for it, still not proposed as a rule (the yield depends on a phrase choice the discipline itself doesn't constrain, so enforcing it would mint compliant runs that find nothing and read as clearance). **Directly contests Daedalus's "first clean round" framing:** *"He said one clean round is one clean round and he'd want a second before anyone reads it as a trend. This is the second fire in the arm and it found something."* Not a code defect — Round 77 changed nothing — but a defect in the argument, the same standing Round 77 gave its own §5. Opened a systematic sweep of never-reopened single-commit instrument files (the cross-pollination brief's own suggested mitigation, applied rather than left as a suggestion): read `scripts/lib/recall-recogniser.mjs` in full (177 lines, unopened since Round 58), checked three load-bearing claims against today's producer — all three hold — traced by hand (not executed) a fourth failure direction and surfaced, without filing, a compounding case the file's own docblock warns against overclaiming from; four more single-commit files flagged, still unopened. **Neither 🔴 moves; the five-opus-run distance-arm go/no-go is untouched — eight consecutive fires across two agents finding defects in instruments, producers, prose, and now a demonstration's own tree-provenance, rather than in data, still not a reason to run one.** Both fires zero API spend, zero live runs, no server started, no code changed. Suite reconfirmed unchanged both fires: server **1423/1423 (86 files)**, client **239/239 (13 skipped)**, typecheck clean, three workspaces. - **Round 79/80, 2026-08-23 (Daedalus MID/WORK fire 13:17 PT, Theseus WORK fire 14:47 PT, 0 live calls, 0 API spend both) — the positional claim survives, but not for the reason given, and the fix built from that reason is blind on the path that matters more.** Daedalus reproduced every cell of Theseus's Round 78 tree-provenance table this session, not taken on report — all three trees check out — and **conceded without qualification**: the Round 77 §5 grep was validated in the tree where the bug is already fixed, one section after he'd written that a mechanism only firing post-fix is not a guard. He then ran the check implied but not run by either memo: `git blame` on both occurrences of Theseus's replacement string, `"today's producer"` — both are Theseus's own words, one day apart, and `git grep` confirms zero prior occurrences anywhere in `docs/` before Round 69. The one positive case for either noun-based grep rule is same-author coinage reused within 24 hours, not a property of propositions or of shared vocabulary — **agreed with Theseus that neither rule should be enforced, for a second independent reason.** Opened the first of his own five flagged single-commit instrument files, `recall-recogniser.mjs`, and re-examined his own Round 77 §5 claim about `headerExplainsTheEdge`'s positional read: that it survives because both `gapSentences` call sites `parts.join(' ')` into one paragraph. A scratch vitest against the real `buildRecogniser` and `expandConversationRange` — no re-implementation — found **the join is not what carries it**: `join(' ')` guarantees one paragraph only if no element contains a blank line, and the expand path's first element interpolates a channel name straight off the DB, constrained only by `.trim()` (`routes/channels.ts:144`, `queries.ts:162`; the schema at `db/index.ts:45` has no CHECK). A `\n\n` in the name reproduces the failure he'd said the join ruled out; a bare `\n` — untouched by any join — is enough on its own to split every edge line across a per-line regex and zero every count, with `recogniserBlind: false` and `expectationViolations: []` throughout. Named this adjacent to, and not resolving, his own explicitly-unrun `clausesOf` over-split control. Proposed a one-expression fix (`edgeHeaderStem` present in text with `edgeLines === 0` is a contradiction) and **deliberately did not ship it** — sequencing stays xian's. **Theseus (WORK fire, 14:47 PT)** conceded his own stated reason for the same claim in the same terms Daedalus used against himself — "the claim was protected by the corpus, not by the join" — naming it the second time in three rounds a positional claim of his was carried by data rather than the mechanism he cited. Tested the path Daedalus hadn't: **search**, not expand. On search, each excerpt's edge lines carry *that excerpt's own* channel name (`recall.ts:882`, `:901`), so a broken name breaks only its own lines — a two-conversation search with one bad name **halves** `edgeReachable` (6→3) rather than zeroing it, with every guard including Daedalus's own proposed fix reading clean (one conversation stays intact, so `edgeLines` never hits zero). Named the mechanism precisely: `clausesOf`'s own doc comment records how the coverage check's granularity was set after a reworded clause passed a same-shape check while its count silently zeroed — **this is that same defect, one granularity out, reintroduced by the fix meant to close the clause-level version of it.** Measured a line-geometry replacement (count `P.open` openers against `edgeLines + scopeGapLines`) live across seven cases: catches both failures Daedalus's fix misses, produces one false positive on a turn that pastes a marker on its own line — common on a corpus of agents discussing this very tool — filed the tradeoff as a corpus judgement, not a recommendation. Separately closed his own open control from Round 79 (the `clausesOf` over-split can't corrupt a count, only trigger the blind flag — right call not to file it in Round 79, wrong reason recorded for it) and ran one nobody asked for (a `"` in the name correctly triggers `recogniserBlind` — the file works exactly as designed when the *clause* survives as text, the whole contrast with §3, where the *line* stopped existing). **Neither 🔴 moves; the five-opus-run distance-arm go/no-go is untouched — the tenth consecutive fire across two agents finding defects in instruments, producers, prose, a demonstration's own tree-provenance, and now a fix's own blind spot, rather than in data, still not a reason to run one.** Both fires zero API spend, zero live model calls, no server started, one scratch test each written, run, and deleted. Suite reconfirmed unchanged both fires, independently re-run this fire rather than trusted from either memo: server **1423/1423 (86 files)**, client **239/239 (13 skipped)**, `npm run typecheck` clean, three workspaces. - **Round 81/82, 2026-08-23 (Daedalus STOP fire 17:17 PT, Theseus STOP fire 19:47 PT, 0 live calls, 0 API spend both) — the proposed fix is entirely subsumed by the existing check, and the two checks left standing are not one tradeoff but two independent ones, decided by a corpus count that runs opposite the claim that first invoked it.** Daedalus reproduced Theseus's Round 80 rows exactly, then proved (not just conceded) that his own Round 79 proposed fix, §4 (`edgeHeaderStem` present with `edgeLines === 0`), is not a second option at all: `edgeHeaderStem` is emitted iff `edgeGaps > 0` (`recall.ts:615`), and `edgeGaps` itself only accumulates over what renders (`:530-539`) — stem present therefore implies a marker line renders, so **§4 ⟹ §5, structurally, always**. Ran the narrow variant Theseus had left unrun and found his reasoning right but his conclusion wrong: narrow does not fire on `\n` or `\n\n`, but it also does not avoid the false positive it was narrowed for — it requires `P.close`, which the well-formed pasted marker (Theseus's row 7) already has, so narrowing drops both true positives it was meant to keep while leaving the expensive false positive in place. Named a third candidate, **orphan** (a line opening with `P.open`, carrying no `P.close`), ran twelve cases against it, and proved rather than tallied the result: every recognised line is well-formed by construction (`GAP_LINE`/`EDGE_LINE` both anchor `rx(P.close) + '$'`), so `wellFormed ≥ matched` and **`broad ≡ narrow ∨ orphan`**. Reframed what's on xian's desk from one tradeoff between two known-imperfect checks into two independent ones with disjoint catches and disjoint false positives — and found his own noise-floor objection ("this project's transcripts paste these markers constantly") reaches narrow's false positive (the complete pasted marker) but not orphan's. Went looking for his own false positive and found one: a marker straddling the 4,000-char truncation boundary in `formatTranscriptLine` reads as orphan — measured, not recommended, frequency uncounted. **Theseus (STOP fire, 19:47 PT)** independently verified every source citation against the file itself, not Daedalus's quotation of it, and found the identity needs one more lemma than given: `wellFormed ≥ matched` alone doesn't establish `orphan ⟹ broad` unless the two match patterns are disjoint (a line matching both would break it) — verified disjoint, by construction of the two patterns' required suffixes. Found Daedalus's own §1 undersold its reach: the same `gapGaps`/stem guard covers the expand path too, not just search. Found the one real disagreement: §4, though rightly withdrawn as a standalone *option*, is not subsumed as a *false-positive class* — it tests `text.includes(stem)`, and `text` is message content rendered verbatim (`formatTranscriptLine`, `carried-context.ts:258-268`), not just the header line, so a recalled message whose own content happens to contain the stem string fires §4 while `broad` stays silent (`§4 ⇏ broad`) — not hypothetical, the stem string already occurs three times in this project's own `docs/`. Ran the corpus count Daedalus's memo explicitly declined to offer: 1,310 `.md` files under `docs/`, classified using the shipped `buildRecogniser` patterns themselves, no re-typed literals — **narrow's measured false positives: zero** (its only firing shape, a closed-but-unrecognised paste, doesn't occur anywhere in the corpus); **orphan's three false positives are all one shape**, a real marker hard-wrapped by its own author at prose width, splitting `P.open` from `P.close` across lines — including in Daedalus's own 8/15 log file. **The asymmetry Daedalus's §4 claimed is inverted by measurement: it reaches orphan and broad, not narrow.** Corrects Theseus's own Round 80 word "constantly" to measured fact: 7 marker-shaped lines in 1,310 files. Neither treats the corpus proxy as settling it — both explicitly recommend against another round of constructed test rows and toward measuring false positives against real `messages.content`, which neither can reach from a worktree sandbox. **Neither 🔴 moves; the five-opus-run distance-arm go/no-go is untouched — the eleventh and twelfth consecutive fires across two agents finding defects in an instrument's own proposed replacement and in an uncounted noise-floor claim, rather than in data, still not a reason to run one.** Both fires zero API spend, zero live calls, no server started, one scratch vitest/script each, run then deleted before the suite. - **Round 83/84, 2026-08-24 (both START fires, Daedalus 09:27 PT then Theseus 10:47 PT, 0 live calls, 0 API spend both) — the noise-floor count holds with no correction, the fire that measured it created three more of the thing it was measuring, and the "blocked" real-message measurement was never blocked at all.** Daedalus independently rebuilt Theseus's §3 rather than checking it — same predicates, no literal retyped — and reproduced all seven Round 82 numbers exactly against `docs/**.md` at `9558902^` (1,310 files, 7 openers, 4 well-formed, 4 matched, 3 orphans, 818 over cap, 0 cap-inside-marker), plus the stem-occurrence count (3 in 2 files). **No correction to any number in Round 82.** Found the one number Theseus's §2 was missing: the narrow predicate hits **zero times** in the corpus, so §4-broad isn't merely undersold as having a false positive — in this corpus it has *nothing but* false positives (8 occurrences, 6 files, not one true header), upholding Theseus's withdrawal with a stronger version of his own argument. Then ran the same classifier at HEAD, not just at Theseus's tree, and found what neither had predicted: orphans 3→6, stem files 2→6 — and the three new orphans are Daedalus's own Round 82 log, research doc, and memo to Theseus, all produced by the fire that had just measured 3. Named this precisely rather than either overclaiming or dismissing it: confirms the mechanism harder (wrapped quotation is now six-for-six the only orphan producer, including two self-inflicted) but does **not** restore Theseus's withdrawn "constantly" — the growth is agents documenting the defect, not the project's background rate — and concludes the doc corpus can't be used as a stable proxy because it grows in the disputed category in response to being measured. Separately checked Theseus's claim that no `klatch.db` is reachable from either worktree and found a narrower exception: `.testdata/recall-probe.db` exists in his own worktree, but it's empty (0 messages, 1 channel, 1 entity, 0 projects, DDL-only WAL) — a torn-down probe scaffold, not a corpus, so the practical conclusion is unchanged, but the size of the ask shrinks from "build a corpus" to "populate one that already exists." Joint recommendation with Theseus, stated explicitly: stop deciding narrow-vs-orphan from `docs/**.md`; load real `messages.content` rows and re-run the same classifier. **Theseus (START fire, 10:47 PT)** independently reproduced every one of Round 83's numbers with no correction, then corrected Daedalus's §3 *conclusion* rather than its cells: measured the nine `docs/` files that actually landed between Daedalus's Round 82 commit and HEAD and found **orphans +0, stem occurrences +0** across exactly those nine — the growth Daedalus found was real but not inevitable, since Daedalus's own memo deliberately named the shape without reproducing it and succeeded, proving the behaviour is optional, not structural; all of the new orphans and stem occurrences trace to Theseus's own Round 82 fire, not Daedalus's. Then found the corpus both agents had called blocked was never missing: `queries.ts:1313`/`:1323` write `turn.userText`/`turn.assistantText` verbatim into `messages.content`, those come from `ParsedTurn` (`import/parser.ts:86-93`), produced by the shipped `parseClaudeCodeSession` — so the project's own parser turns a session transcript into exactly the strings an import would store, no database or server required — and `exports/sessions/theseus-2026-03-22.jsonl` (3,860,602 bytes of real transcript) has been tracked in git since the initial import, sitting in both agents' sandboxes the whole time. **17 transcripts → 155 real `messages.content` rows, 199,838 chars, mean 1,289 — 0 openers, 0 well-formed, 0 matched, 0 orphans, 0 stem occurrences, 0 cap-inside-marker, 9 rows (5.8%) over the 4,000-char cap.** Ran a positive-control set of synthetic shapes through the identical predicate block first, to rule out a stale-pattern false zero rather than a clean-corpus true zero — both non-zero categories are reachable by the code path that reported 0/155, so the zero is a measurement, not an artifact. One number moves in Daedalus's favour: the docs proxy overstated cap exposure by roughly 11× (818/1,310 = 62.4% of files vs. 9/155 = 5.8% of real rows; both still 0 straddles). Corrected Daedalus's `.testdata/` claim in turn: `.gitignore:33` makes the directory per-worktree by construction, Theseus's own is empty, and Daedalus's worktree is outside Theseus's sandbox to verify — so "a populated db in one worktree is not a shared corpus," and the concrete ask is narrower than either agent's prior framing: a **copy** of `~/klatch-inbound/dbs/klatch-main.db` (2,124 messages / 16 channels, per Pard 2026-08-12) placed into `.testdata/` in **both** worktrees, a file copy rather than a build, reachable only by xian since the source sits outside every agent's sandbox. Named the honest limit of the transcript already measured: dated 2026-03-22, markers landed 2026-08-15, so it can contain no true positive — a clean noise-floor corpus and a useless true-positive one, the right-shaped limitation for the question actually in dispute. **Neither 🔴 moves, but the ask underneath it changed shape: the five-opus-run distance-arm go/no-go has been replaced by a one-file-copy unblock, and the in-sandbox half of the measurement is done and reads 0 of 155 — the thirteenth and fourteenth consecutive fires across two agents finding defects in a corpus proxy and in each other's framing of their own results, and the first of the fourteen to end in a concrete, small, named action rather than another round.** Both fires zero API spend, zero live calls, no server started, two-to-four scratch scripts each, run then deleted before the suite. Suite reconfirmed unchanged both fires, independently re-run this fire rather than trusted from either memo: server **1423/1423 (86 files)**, client **239/239 (13 skipped)**, `npm run typecheck` clean, three workspaces. Docs: `docs/research/round83-every-number-reproduces-and-measuring-the-floor-raised-it-2026-08-24.md`, `docs/research/round84-the-blocked-measurement-was-in-the-repo-and-it-reads-zero-2026-08-24.md`.
- **Round 85/86, 2026-08-24 (Daedalus MID/WORK fire 13:17 PT, Theseus WORK fire 14:47 PT, 0 live calls, 0 API spend both) — the recognising predicate is proven column-zero-blind for four rounds but the floor it governs could not have moved; the ask both agents have carried since Round 83 is withdrawn, because the DB it asked for was tracked in git the whole time and reads zero at 2,652 rows.** Daedalus reproduced Round 84's eleven cells exactly (no correction), then ran the control neither prior round had: the shipped parser retains only 4.86% of raw transcript bytes, so he re-ran the classifier over the full, unparsed corpus — all 17 files, 4,112,645 raw bytes, no filtering — and got 0 openers/matched/orphans/stem, proving the zero survives at 100% of bytes rather than being an artifact of the 95% the parser discards. Reconstructing both forms of the opener pattern at two refs, he found the predicate this whole arm has run only recognises a marker at the **start of a line** — blind to one quoted mid-sentence — and that 20 of the 26 broad-form orphans at HEAD are exactly that blind spot, including his own Round 83 doc quoting a marker in prose; this withdraws Round 83 §3's self-contamination claim (not self-contaminating, predicate-blind) and is filed as new, not a correction to any published number. Corrected the denominator, not the zero: 143 of 155 rows (99.7% of chars) are one file, one author, one continuous session (2026-03-11 to 2026-03-22) — true n is 1 session, not 155 independent trials, which doesn't dent the conclusion but makes the DB ask more justified rather than less. Found the design's own docblock on the cap constant (`carried-context.ts:66-75`) already predicted ~92% under cap / ~8% over, against the real corpus's measured 5.8% — retiring the docs-corpus comparison on Theseus's own Round 84 reasoning. Built durable tooling so the rebuild-from-scratch discipline survives past this arm: `scripts/measure-marker-floor.mjs` (three corpus modes, positive control gates every run and blocks reporting on failure, DBs opened readonly, `better-sqlite3` imported lazily), `scripts/lib/marker-floor.mjs` (the classifier, extracted so a test certifies the code the script runs), and a 6-test suite. **Theseus (WORK fire, 14:47 PT)** reproduced Round 85 on a second, independently-assembled instrument (patterns built from `P` directly, not imported from Daedalus's module), all cells identical, one commit-hash correction that doesn't change the conclusion. Then closed Daedalus's own §3 finding at the source: `GAP_LINE`/`EDGE_LINE` are `^…$`-anchored on the trimmed line, so `read` structurally implies the narrow branch also fires too — verified empirically (0/0, 0/0, 4/4), adversarially (a space-prefixed marker reads narrow 0/0, broad 1/0), and arithmetically on Daedalus's own table (26 − 6 = 20 = 30 − 10) — **the floor is `matched`, and it is predicate-invariant; nothing in Rounds 82–85 that was a floor number could have moved.** What the half-blindness governs instead is the orphan column, and there it's sharper than "one pattern": of the 20 broad-only lines, 17 carry an intact close on the same line — embedded, not severed, a different failure mechanism from the 6 narrow orphans (all one signature, a marker genuinely wrapped across lines, opener at column 0, digit, no close) — proposed but deliberately did not land a three-count taxonomy (`read`/`severed`/`embedded`/residue) in Daedalus's own file, leaving the choice to him. **Then found the ask itself was already answered:** `backups/klatch.db.backup-2026-03-14` — 5,230,592 bytes, tracked in git, in every sandbox, and named in the very docblock both agents had just finished quoting (`carried-context.ts:30-32`) — is the populated klatch DB four rounds have been asking xian to provide. Measured, cross-checked identical against the shipped script: 2,652 rows, 139 channels, 2,823,903 chars, **0/0/0 narrow, 0/0/0 broad, 0 stem, 0 straddles**, 90 over cap (3.4%). Rule-of-three bound tightens from ~2.1% to ~0.11%; honest limit unchanged and now explicit — the backup is dated 2026-03-14, markers landed 2026-08-15, so it can hold no true positive by construction, same limitation as the transcripts, at 17× the size. **`~/klatch-inbound/dbs/klatch-main.db` is formally withdrawn as a lever — off both agents' lists, nothing on this arm waiting on xian.** Corrected Daedalus's §5 in turn: three of the docblock's four numbers are exact measurements against the real corpus (median 580, p90 2,334, max 64,627) but its fourth, "~92% are under this," is off by 4.5 points (measured 96.5%) — the design decision is untouched either way, and safer at the lower true rate. **Neither 🔴 moves; the ask underneath the eviction item — narrowed in Round 83/84 from a five-opus-run measurement plan to a one-file copy — narrows again to nothing: every corpus reachable from inside either sandbox now reads zero, and the distance-arm go/no-go remains entirely xian's, unblocked by any further data-gathering — the fifteenth and sixteenth consecutive fires across two agents finding defects in a predicate, a denominator, and their own shared blind spot to a corpus that was in the repo the whole time, rather than in data.** Both fires zero API spend, zero live calls, no server started; Theseus's five scratch `.mts` deleted before commit, Daedalus's tooling kept as the one artifact meant to survive the arm. Suite reconfirmed unchanged, independently re-run this fire, not trusted from either memo: server **1429/1429 (87 files)**, client **239/239 (13 skipped)**; `npm run typecheck` clean, three workspaces. - **Round 87/88, 2026-08-24 (Daedalus STOP fire ~17:1x PT, Theseus STOP fire 19:47 PT, 0 live calls, 0 API spend both) — the five-category partition lands with no correction to any number, `backups/*` turns out to hold two tracked files not one, and a mode built to stop hand-maintaining the corpus list finds four containers it cannot itself read, all four measuring zero.** Daedalus checked Theseus's Round 86 §2 at the anchor rather than re-running (`recall-recogniser.mjs:41,51` both `^`-anchored, confirming `matchedAnywhere ≡ matched` is a pattern property) and conceded §3 in full — summing quoting with severing had overstated the wrapping defect ~4×. Landed `read`/`severed`/`unparsed`/`embedded`/`residue`, five not four, because an anchored, closed, unread line has nowhere to go in a four-bucket scheme and reads 0 everywhere measured — the argument for naming it. All six Round 82–85 columns still derived from the five rather than remeasured; `docs/**.md` at HEAD reproduces Theseus's hand classification exactly (4/6/0/17/3). Found `git ls-files -- 'backups/*'` returns **two** files — the second, `…pre-fresh` (335,872 bytes), measures 219 rows / 0 in all five / 4 over cap — the third consecutive round a tracked corpus was missing from someone's list (the 17 transcripts, then the March DB, now this). Built `--all-tracked` (every tracked file, raw bytes, no parser, no extension list) to stop maintaining that list by hand: **1,659 files, 28,053,136 chars, 0 `unparsed` across every tracked byte**, and it surfaced that `fixtures/claude-ai/`'s three JSON conversations and two zip exports — feeding a *different* shipped parser than the `*.jsonl` glob eleven rounds had checked — were never in scope. Tests 6→12: partition property, `matchedAnywhere === matched` as an invariant, legacy derivation, digitless, tally re-derivation, generator acceptance. **Theseus** reproduced every Round 87 cell exactly on a corpus that had grown by three files in the interim (+0 in every classified cell), then answered Daedalus's own closing question: `--all-tracked` enumerates every tracked file but `readFileSync(f,'utf8')` cannot see DEFLATE. Constructed a control with the fixtures' own `AdmZip` writer — a marker built from `P` reads `embedded=1` loose in JSON and **0 in all five once zipped**; against the real fixtures, **0 of 17** and **0 of 29** inflated lines are findable in the raw decode, a total loss, and both fixtures drive `packages/server/src/import/claude-ai-zip.ts` into `messages.content` rows in production. Named a fourth candidate container (`research/….jsonl.zip`) and, checking it before it reached the memo, found it byte-identical (sha256-confirmed) to a `.jsonl` already tracked loose beside it and already among the 17 — corrected himself rather than publishing a fourth corpus that wasn't one. **All four opaque containers — two fixture zips, the near-miss zip, and a `.docx` — read zero in all five categories; no floor number in Rounds 82–88 moves.** Separately corrected the mode's own byte figure: `Buffer.byteLength` after a lossy UTF-8 decode turns every invalid byte into three (U+FFFD), so the printed total runs **+11.2%** over the true 28,532,740 on-disk bytes — 27 of 1,662 files (34.0% of tracked byte mass) decode lossily, mostly harmless (two SQLite backups, 19 PNGs, an MP4) but one is a tracked *source* test file whose bytes aren't valid UTF-8, flagged not chased. Proposed three instrument changes (print an `opaque` file count, report on-disk bytes beside decoded weight, narrow the coverage claim to file-level) and deliberately did not land them same-day, in the same instrument, without Daedalus having seen the argument — left it his call. **Both agents state directly that this arm has no further in-sandbox measurement worth a fire: enumeration is closed at the file level, open at the byte level by a bounded, fully-measured 4 files / 260,205 inflated chars, all zero, and the distance-arm go/no-go remains entirely xian's.** Both fires zero API spend, zero live calls, no server started, no product code changed either round. Suite independently reconfirmed this fire: server **1435/1435 (87 files)**, client **239/239 (13 skipped)**, `npm run typecheck` clean, three workspaces. - **Round 89/90, 2026-08-25 (both START fires, Daedalus 09:17 PT then Theseus 10:47 PT, 0 live calls, 0 API spend both) — Round 88's three proposed instrument changes land plus a fourth, more serious one found by accident: the compliance check both agents run every fire can pass while measuring nothing, and the one item Round 89 left open closes by mutation-testing the fix itself.** Daedalus landed all three of Theseus's Round 88 §5 asks in his own file — `opaque` printed as a measured count (**4**), on-disk bytes reported beside decoded weight, the coverage sentence narrowed to file-level — plus a fourth he wasn't looking for: run from a shell whose cwd had drifted into `packages/server/`, `--docs WORKTREE` printed a complete, well-formed, all-zero report, indistinguishable from a genuinely clean corpus, because `git ls-files -- docs` resolves relative to cwd and matched nothing. Both enumerating modes now refuse to report on an empty file list and exit 3. Distinguished detecting a container (`opaque`, by leading magic bytes) from detecting whether its text is reachable (walking zip local file headers and counting entries actually compressed, not stored) — the tracked `.docx` reads 18/22 entries compressed by this route, matching Theseus's count of 4 opaque files by a method that can tell a container from an unreadable one; an incomplete walk (general-purpose flag bit 3 set, sizes in a trailing data descriptor) is reported as "1/1+" rather than a false "1/1". Corrected Round 88 §4: the flagged non-UTF-8 source file is valid UTF-8 — a byte round-trip is exact — carrying three literal U+FFFD characters as content (a prior lossy edit committed verbatim), not decode loss; narrows "27 files decode lossily" to 26, leaves the 34.0%/33.8% byte-mass figure unaffected. Built `round89-opaque-containers.test.ts`, six bidirectional tests, the load-bearing one asserting a marker deflated into a zip entry is absent as a substring while the identical marker stored uncompressed reads `embedded=1` — without both halves the all-zero reading would be consistent with the marker never existing. Flagged, not claimed: attempted a mutation check on the new controls, the harness declined in-place edits to a tracked file, left as a structural argument rather than a measurement. **Theseus (START fire, 10:47 PT)** closed exactly that gap by building a harness in `.testdata/` (gitignored scratch) that copies the module, mutates the copy, and points a scratch copy of the test at it via one rewritten import — no tracked file touched at any point. Ran Daedalus's three named mutants plus four of his own: 6 of 7 killed first pass. **M5 — deleting the flag-bit-3 early-return guard — survived**, and diffing real-vs-mutant classification across all 1,673 tracked files found it observably wrong on exactly one: the real `research/….jsonl.zip` reads `1/1+, complete:false`; the mutant reads the same counts with `complete:true` — a floor silently reported as a total, the same shape as the defect `--all-tracked` exists to remove, one level further in, and zero of six assertions caught it. Added one control (two zip entries written, first with bit 3 set and header sizes zeroed, asserting the walk reports one, not two) — full matrix now **7/7 killed**, no other coverage weakened — and landed it on his own initiative, citing Daedalus's own Round 88 precedent for landing an additive, in-seat fix rather than a round-trip. Independently reproduced Round 89 §2 (container table, opaque = 4, same files and counts) and §4 (empty-corpus guard, exit 3, positive control still green, no table) exactly. One correction: Daedalus's predicted "1,341 files" undercounted his own session log, which the mode itself enumerates — actual **1,342**, one file, in the direction of a file he wrote; every other predicted cell landed exactly (4/6/0/17/3, stem 7, `+0`). A smaller note: `measure-marker-floor.mjs` requires `npx tsx`, not bare `node`, which fails at module resolution before reaching any corpus — Round 89 §4 reproduces fine under `tsx`, flagged so a bare-`node` re-run isn't read as the same failure. **Both agents restate there is no further in-sandbox measurement worth a fire on this arm — the distance-arm go/no-go remains entirely xian's, unblocked by any further measurement.** Independently re-verified this fire, not trusted from either memo: server **1442/1442 (88 files)**, client **239/239 (13 skipped)**, matching Theseus's claimed count exactly (1441 + 1); `npm run typecheck` clean, three workspaces. - **Round 91–94, 2026-08-25 — xian said GO, the arm ran, and the headline number landed through a mechanism neither agent had registered; the primary question the arm was built to answer is still unmeasured.** **Round 91 (Daedalus)** pointed Theseus's own Round 90 mutation technique at his own module and found two more ways `complete` could report a finish that was really an unresolved stop (zip64 sizes, a truncated tail) — one of them, on the tracked `.docx`, would have printed `opaque: false` for an archive whose text is genuinely unreachable, "the unfalsifiable zero this module exists to stop printing, rebuilt inside the module." Fixing it the honest way (a positive completion check) incidentally made Theseus's own Round 90 M5 control pass on its own mutant; caught before landing, a replacement control (`entries`) pins the same defect one layer down. Full matrix 9/9 after two more survivors led to deleting a redundant branch rather than patching around it — no product code number moved. **Xian's GO landed the same day, ~14:15 PT, relayed by Janus**: run the distance arm, ~5 opus runs, ~80 test rows — plus his own framing that the dominant deployment shape (one human, own agents) makes a residual eviction gap lower-stakes than a cross-tenant one, a threat-model clarification rather than a scope directive (`memo-janus-to-daedalus-theseus-cc-calliope-xian-distance-arm-go-2026-08-25.md`). **Round 92 (Daedalus)** built arm Q against that go-ahead without spending it: 80 rows, restriction at offered-start +15 — past the +10 read-appetite ceiling observed on record — eviction margin 1 row, the tightest arm yet, stop-rule written into the docblock before any run. `--dry` reproduced N1's Round 63 registration byte-for-byte and landed every one of Q's own pre-registered ordinals exactly against the real `buildCarriedContext` output. **Round 93 (Theseus)** answered Daedalus's own open question same day, before the spend: whether "+6…+10" is a row count, a character budget, or a fraction of the offer doesn't need resolving, because all three readings clear Q's +15 restriction (a new `verify-appetite-readings.mjs`, derived from the seeded rows rather than hard-coded, confirmed live rather than assumed); also found the offer-size change wasn't a choice but entailed by the algebra that makes the arm feasible at all (`markOffset = 2G-1`, `trailWidth = 2F+3`, `G ≤ F-9` pin the offer to ≥37 rows once the restriction sits at +15). **Round 94 (Theseus) spent the five runs.** Headline: **Q caught the restriction 1/5 — exactly Round 92 §6's pre-registered figure.** But the number is right for a reason nobody registered: four of five runs never expanded at all, and the fifth took the whole offer verbatim, so there are **zero partial reads** — the read-appetite question the whole arm exists to measure has zero datapoints, and Round 92's six-cell clearance table is neither confirmed nor refuted by this run. Found not from the summary line but from walking the raw artifacts and noticing `expandAction.startPlusNs` was empty in four of five files — "had §6 read 'expect 4/5 partial reads stopping in +6…+13,' the mismatch would have been visible in the summary table instead of requiring a walk through the JSON," Theseus's own words, naming his own Round 92 pre-registration as sharing the defect. Within Q, expansion perfectly separates on one variable: the model expanded iff its second search missed (9-row hit → 0/4 expanded; 0-row miss → 1/1). Flagged as hypothesis, not claimed: the four no-expand runs may have found a *condition-shaped decoy* in the restate pair (a benign note they all read as "one related note from the same thread") and stopped without ever opening the offer — a cheaper, more dangerous false-clearance route than the short-read model Rounds 56–63 have been testing, because no partial read happens at all. **Cannot be promoted past hypothesis for a records reason, not a reasoning one**: the restate pair exists in N1 too, and N1's own second-search-hit runs re-rendered it and expanded anyway — but N1's second-query strings no longer exist (`.testdata/` is gitignored, Round 63's live JSONs are gone), so which query reached the pair in each arm can't be checked. Named as the same instrument defect the "commit live-round raw JSONs" 🔴 item already asks xian to rule on (below) — this time it isn't a published count that can't be checked, it's an entire mechanism. Proposed fix, general rather than ad hoc: every query string of every call goes in the round doc going forward. No product code touched either round; no instrument edits in the same fire that produced a result on it. Open ask, explicitly a judgement call and routed to Daedalus, not xian: is the "decoy arm" (Q's geometry, the restate pair's condition-shaped wording removed) worth five more turns — nothing on this outstanding requires xian directly. Suite reconfirmed each round, server **1447/1447 (88 files)**, client **239/239 (13 skipped)**, typecheck clean ×3 workspaces — unchanged across all four rounds (Round 92/93/94 changed no file under `packages/`; Round 91 was a same-file instrument fix in `scripts/`). - **Round 95/96, 2026-08-26 (both START fires, Daedalus 09:19 PT then Theseus, 0 live calls, one `--dry` run) — Arm R is built to spec and dry-clean, a transcription done just in time finds the decoy was in every run including the one that expanded, and there is now an explicit GO ask for xian.** **Round 95 (Daedalus)** agreed the decoy arm is worth building, on a sharper ground than Theseus's own: it doubles as the retry of Q's own unmeasured primary DV (read appetite), so either outcome informs — a null here is also evidence the suppressor is geometric, not verbal, which would have to be established before any third distance arm. Pinned two build constraints before any code moved: **`restateUser` must keep the literal codeword and drop only the naming instruction** — stripping the codeword too would collapse the 9-row search neighbourhood into a different one in the same arm, reproducing Q's own confound (an expansion becoming uninterpretable, geometry and wording changed together, "Q's failure mode reproduced precisely"); and **`restateAck`** (the assistant's own echoed compliance) **should be stripped alongside it**, on the theory it's plausibly the stronger half of the decoy. Also noted `restateUser` is byte-identical across every existing arm, so a decoy-free arm's read *depth* isn't comparable to the calibrated `+6…+10` band — the valid DV is binary (does the read reach +15), not a depth shift. Found Round 94 scored itself against only the most recent memo's prediction, missing an earlier, sharper one written directly into the harness comment (`probe-recall-tool.mjs:978`, "expand rate: unchanged from N1") — checked against N1's actual 5/5 vs. Q's 1/5, that prediction is **refuted**, not merely unscored, and Round 94 didn't say so; proposed the round doc score every pre-registration per arm as held/refuted/untested going forward. Flagged a possible clock: Round 94 §4's decoy hypothesis rests on the five runs' actual reply text, which lives only in gitignored `.testdata/` JSON and not in the round doc — couldn't check from his own worktree (no R94 files there) and asked Theseus to transcribe before they might not survive to be read. Named for xian directly: the "warn users a long transcript may not be fully read" mitigation he favours doesn't cover the decoy route at all, since the agent never opens the long offer — a user told "it might not read everything" would reasonably conclude a *short*-seeming transcript is safe, which on this account isn't what governs. **Round 96 (Theseus)** did the transcription first — files still present — and it is what surfaced the round's real finding: **the decoy token is in the carried context of all five Round 94 runs, not a subset** — `CARRIED_CONTEXT_MAX_MESSAGES=20` over Q's 80 rows carries rows 61-80, which includes the restate pair, on every run — and the one run that expanded (R94L3) had it too, read the offer anyway, and found and held the restriction; it could only have known the naming instruction's exact wording from carried context, since none of its own tool calls returned that text. This narrows the decoy hypothesis from "presence suppresses" to "a hit on the model's own targeted query does, ambient carried presence doesn't" — one data point on the non-suppression side, n=1. Consequence for the arm: because `restateUser` sits inside both the carried window and the search neighbourhood, editing it removes the decoy from both at once — Arm R tests the conjunction of the two channels, not either alone; separating them needs moving the token out of row 79 entirely, which changes the geometry Round 92 fixed and is a design conversation, not this arm. Second finding: the registered null ("suppressor is Q's 80-row length") isn't the only one that survives — the neighbourhood's second excerpt is also the conversation's *flush-terminal* one (last excerpt, no trailing "later messages" edge) and opens with wording that reads as the end of the thread — a second, cheaper-to-test candidate, registered this time alongside the first rather than left implicit. Built Arm R exactly to Daedalus's spec (codeword kept, naming instruction and `restateAck` both stripped); `--dry` confirms every registered ordinal identical to Q's (fact/marking positions, min distance, both excerpts, restriction at +15) — the only number that moves is carried-context size (3815→3746, a 69-byte delta matching the removed text exactly). Also found and fixed a second instance of Round 94's own transcription gap: its doc recorded the "marking absent" prompt-gate line but dropped the "fact present" one — the dropped line is the one that would have shown Finding 1 five days earlier. Swept `docs/` for leaked `` </content> ``/`` </invoke> `` tool-artifact text after finding two instances in 8/25 files; both removed, both his own. **Explicit ask for xian, distinct from the standing distance-arm GO and not assumed to be covered by it:** authorize 5 live opus runs for Arm R, same shape and cost as Round 94's. - **Round 97/98, 2026-08-26 (both WORK fires, Daedalus then Theseus, 0 live calls) — the free pre-spend check ran, broke the argument made for it, and left the GO ask the same size but on firmer footing.** **Round 97 (Daedalus)** verified Theseus's Arm R build field-by-field against Round 96's spec — byte-identical except the three printed-not-seeded fields and the two restate fields, exactly as claimed. Then argued no to Round 96's registered flush-terminal-excerpt survivor: N1's own `--dry` artifacts show its second excerpt is flush-terminal too, and N1 expanded 5/5 — "a feature constant across the split can't explain the split," Round 96's own move turned back on it. Separately caught that keeping the token in seq 79 is load-bearing in a way neither prior memo said — it's the *only* occurrence inside the carried-context window, so stripping it would flip `promptHoldsToken` false and void the arm outright, not merely shift the geometry. Found Round 94's distance reading is confounded with offer width, not distance alone (the algebra `trailing width = offset + 2T` means the two move together across every existing arm), and named a `scopedTotal`-based warning ("a long transcript may not be fully read") as unobservable to the agent, since nothing in the rendered tool output ever states the conversation's total length. Proposed a free, zero-spend check before xian rules: Round 63 has the transcripts to say whether N1's runs *actually retrieved* the flush-terminal excerpt on their second query; if they did and still expanded, retrieval-framing is refuted at n=5 and R's null is nearly certain, making the five turns low-information. **Round 98 (Theseus, 0 live calls)** ran it, and it cuts both ways. N1 never retrieved the excerpt — Daedalus's favourable conditional holds, R's premise is intact. But the same check breaks Daedalus's own refutation: N1's flush-terminal edge was a `--dry` *prediction*, absent from N1's live decision render entirely, so there was no live instance of it for the 5/5 to be evidence about — "a structural prediction read as an observation," a third instance in four days of the same error class, this one his own to claim as much as Daedalus's. Going back to Q's own artifacts (not N1's) surfaced the round's real finding: **the model's second search query decided the outcome in all ten runs on record, no exception** — four runs whose second query hit the decoy's own token got a two-excerpt, decoy-marked, flush-terminal render and did not expand (0/4); six runs whose second query missed that token (five of N1, one of Q) got no such render and expanded regardless (6/6) — including one run that reproduced the naming instruction verbatim despite never being shown rows 74-80 by any tool call, sourced from carried context alone. "Presence doesn't suppress" survives sharper than either agent had it, but the decoy and the missing trailing edge are now shown to be confounded together in every observed instance — R holds both constant and varies only the decoy's text, so the writeup can't claim to separate them, only to test the conjunction. Registered a scoring rule for R before any spend: score over the conditional denominator (runs whose second call actually returns the two-excerpt render), report both `k/n_conditional` and `k/5` always, declare the arm underpowered below 3-of-5 reaching that branch, and log every call-2 query string verbatim going forward — the single most predictive observable across both arms, never previously tabulated. **The GO ask does not change size**: Theseus's own words, "nothing changed about the size of the ask: 5 live opus runs, arm R, already built and gated" — his read is that the free check strengthened the case to run it rather than weakened it. Daedalus's alternative, an "Arm S" that de-confounds offer width from distance by holding Q's live trailing width constant, is arithmetic only, not built, and offered as his preference for a *second* GO if xian grants one — not a substitute for the standing ask. Direction is the safe one (corpus-absent over-states what didn't run) but it compounds silently; fixed by deriving the count from each mutant's own `expect` field — 16 both ways, confirmed by running it, not just by arithmetic. Visible only from a corpus-free worktree — Round 104's own case B, built to reproduce that worktree, simulates absence for the *target* verifier, not for the harness's own denominator, "the simulation covers the target and not the instrument." The secondary (an explicit "no restriction" claim) moved in the predicted direction but only at n=5 (Q 3/5, R 1/5) — named, not claimed. `premiseRenderHeld` executed live for the first time (5/5 decidable, no undecided path) and the fabricated-reconstruction sweep Daedalus warned about did not occur (0 of 25 calls across both corpora). An ordinal-free premise holds 5/5 in R and 4/5 in Q — computed, reported, and deliberately not used to re-score, per Round 104's own pre-registration rule that a premise failure is a finding about the instrument, not a license to re-score under a rule chosen after seeing the outcomes. - **Round 107–112, 2026-08-28 (six fires across two seats, START through STOP, zero live spend throughout) — Round 98's "ten of ten, no exception" rule breaks under Arm R, an ordinal-free replacement survives every falsifier thrown at it, and the arm built to test *which* exposure rule is right (arm S) turns out, by a defect in its own designer's exclusion clause, to test only *whether* exposure matters at all — and then that "0 of 10" number itself turns out to be computed under a clause the same commit had already repealed.** **Round 107 (Daedalus)** built the self-check `verify-verifier-exit-codes.mjs` case D discussed in Round 105/106 (a corpus-free copy plus a self-mutation that must move the denominator), confirming `19/19`'s arithmetic; then scored Round 98's rule against Round 106's own Arm R table and found it breaks — 2 of R's 5 runs contradict it, dropping the cross-corpus score from "10/10, no exception" to 12/15 — and proposed an ordinal-free replacement ("expands iff no call ever rendered the two-excerpt neighbourhood") scoring 14/15, naming its own falsifier (an unread N1 artifact) before leaning on it. **Round 108 (Theseus)** confirmed `19/19` live, then found the N1 artifacts are gone from *both* seats (deleted at Round 63's own fire) — but Round 63's per-call table is call-complete (17/17 calls, header-verified) and contains no two-excerpt render on any ordinal, so the falsifier doesn't fire and 14/15 stands; proposed a "recency" refinement, scored it worse (8/10) than the ordinal-free rule, killed by his own two hardest runs in the same fire. **Round 109 (Daedalus)** independently re-derived `19/19` from the opposite corpus state (8 ran + 11 not-run = 19) and re-derived the falsifier-check rather than trusting Theseus's report, both confirmed; then found a completeness defect in his own prior round — a table captioned "every call in both corpora" that held only one — and produced the arm-S cumulative-exposure pre-registration plus standing rule 11 (a finished arm can only serve as a new design's cell if its geometry matches on every dimension). **Round 110 (Theseus)** corrected his own file-count error (25, not 27 — conclusion unaffected), recovered and printed the Q-corpus per-call table Daedalus had assumed unrecoverable, and scored all three rival rules mechanically on all ten live runs: ordinal 7/10, ordinal-free 9/10, recency 8/10 — finding Q doesn't discriminate any better than N1 does, and that **all** discrimination among the three rules lives in exactly three of the ten runs (R L1, R L2, R L5). Net: arm S's Q2 value is "unmeasured and flagged," not zero — filed as standing rule 14 (recompute every number a narrowed clause produced, in the same commit). **No GO requested or implied at any point in this chain; no product code touched (`packages/` untouched throughout, `scripts/`/`docs/research/`-only); suite independently re-run at each checkpoint, unchanged: server 1447/1447 (88 files), client 239/239 (13 skipped), typecheck clean ×3.** Docs: `docs/research/round107-the-instrument-checks-its-own-denominator-and-arm-r-breaks-round-98s-ten-of-ten-2026-08-28.md`, `docs/research/round108-19-of-19-passes-the-n1-artifacts-are-gone-from-both-seats-and-my-own-recency-rule-died-in-the-same-fire-2026-08-28.md`, `docs/research/round109-the-falsifier-does-not-fire-verified-here-and-round-106s-both-corpora-block-holds-one-2026-08-28.md`, `docs/research/round110-the-q-half-is-recoverable-and-the-rules-disagree-on-three-runs-not-ten-2026-08-28.md`, `docs/research/round111-arm-s-as-registered-has-a-rule-12-number-of-zero-and-my-own-void-clause-is-why-2026-08-28.md`, `docs/research/round112-the-transcription-holds-and-the-zero-is-computed-from-a-clause-the-same-commit-repealed-2026-08-28.md`, `docs/research/arm-s-cumulative-exposure-preregistration-2026-08-28.md`.
- **Round 113–114, 2026-08-29 (both START fires, Daedalus then Theseus, zero live spend) — the correction chain finds its own defects one level up from the last correction, twice, and the trigger for merging rules 12–15 fires.** **Round 113 (Daedalus)** accepted Round 112 outright, independently reproducing its 10-of-10 transcription, then applied rule 14 the mechanical way (grep the stale value, don't re-read) and found three more survivals of the superseded "zero" in the pre-registration — one in a section heading, one inside the amending text that itself superseded it, one downstream in a recommendation. Turned the same check on Theseus's own recompute and found it, too, substituted a proxy: the void clause reads whether a query was productive and whether a *second distinct neighbourhood* rendered, and neither `sep === 0` (Daedalus's Round 111 proxy) nor `seps.some(s >= 1)` (Theseus's Round 112 proxy) is in that alphabet. Re-enumerating over render kinds carrying `productive`/`neighbourhood` rather than `sep`: **all 10 of 10** discriminating shapes are ambiguous, not 7 of 10 — including `[1,0]`, the one shape the live corpus produced twice. Split S-unexposed's "zero" into two claims by gate: geometric (0 discriminating shapes) only if gate 2 holds; if a breach is reachable, 78 of 90 shapes discriminate and the exclusion clause removes every one — clause-covered, not geometric, under a breach neither agent has priced. Re-priced arm T against the corrected S (was: T 15/15 vs. S 0/10; is: T 15/15 vs. S's 10 surviving-but-ambiguous shapes) — real margin, much smaller, conditional on gate 2's still-undetermined buildability — and found the spend-halving option (B, five exposed runs) keeps 100% of option A's Q2 power, reversing the prior recommendation. Filed **rule 15**: every field a void/flag/exclude clause's antecedent names must exist in the per-run record, or the clause is unscoreable by construction — the arm-S schema (`seps[]`) never contained the fields (`rows[]`, `neighbourhoods[]`, `productive[]`) either proxy needed, so both authors were scoring the only thing the schema let them compute. Named the pattern explicitly: rules 13, 14 and 15 are three views of one failure, and a fourth view should trigger a merge rather than a rule 16. **Round 114 (Theseus)** reimplemented rather than read Round 113's arithmetic and reproduced it exactly (85/62/10 shapes; the 80/0/0 vs. 90/78/0 gate-2 split), agreeing the durable claim is geometric-given-gate-2, clause-covered otherwise. But found the new kind Round 113's 10-of-10 depends on (`X0`: a productive `sep 0` render reaching a neighbourhood not already shown) has **zero witnesses across all ten live runs** — every `sep 0` render showed the same rows (39–43) as every other, and no query in the registered set ever reached the new region (77–80) without also re-showing the old one. `X1` (the mirror kind behind the other 7 shapes) is attested seven times and unaffected. Net: **7 of 10 is what the corpus attests, 10 of 10 is what a conservative reading assumes**, and the difference rides on whether `X0` is reachable at all (unchecked, added to §6 next to gate 2's buildability). The surviving-shapes headline stays at 10 either way, since a voiding kind only ever removes shapes, never adds survivors — so Round 113's §5 re-pricing is untouched by this finding. Declined to revert the pre-registration to 7 on the corpus's silence alone, naming that move the exact intuition-substitution rule 12 exists to block, and registered "10 conservative, 7 witnessed" instead. Identified this exact split as the fourth view Round 113 flagged as the merge trigger, and drafted — without committing — a merge of rules 12–15 into one rule with four checkpoints (before-spend, write-time, amend-time), held for Daedalus's sign-off since he owns rules 13 and 15. Own rule-14 sweep of Round 113's commit found "ambiguous" stale in two more places, one a heading, for the second consecutive round a stale heading survived its own correcting commit — and caught a miscount in his own new verifier (predicted 6 attested `X1`-like renders, derivation returned 7) before shipping it. **No GO requested or implied at any point; `packages/` untouched both rounds; no live spend.** Docs: `docs/research/round113-the-recompute-used-a-proxy-too-and-the-unexposed-zero-is-conditional-on-a-gate-2026-08-29.md`, `docs/research/round114-the-kind-that-carries-the-correction-has-zero-witnesses-and-ambiguous-is-already-stale-2026-08-29.md`.
- **Round 115–116, 2026-08-29 (both MID fires, Daedalus then Theseus, zero live spend) — the 10-vs-7 dispute was never a corpus question, and the cell the fix was copied from turns out to have two ungated assertions of its own; the rules merge lands as rule 16.** **Round 115 (Daedalus)** found that the pre-registration's §1 already asserts the property that would settle `X0`'s reachability — S-exposed's token-bearing neighbourhood is "the *only* productive query" — and §3's gate list never checks that assertion, though it checks the exactly analogous one for S-unexposed (gate 2). Under the assertion, S-exposed's ambiguity is **0**, not Round 113's 10 or Round 114's 7 — both were properties of an alphabet mixing in-cell kinds with breach kinds, not properties of the cell. Proposed **gate 1b** to close it; verified the surviving 10 shapes are set-identical (not just equal in count) across the split, so nothing downstream moves. Read Round 114's zero-witnesses finding as a fact about the gate rather than a base rate: in the two corpus runs whose first render actually matches S-exposed's shape, neither has a second `sep >= 1` render at all, so gate 1b is **entailed by gate 1** in any two-region geometry — converting the open item from a probability into a region count, still undetermined for arm S. Against his own position: the "ambiguous" limb of arm T's pricing was never a real margin, and S now carries a second underived pre-spend condition, a real cost not netted to zero. Signed off on merging rules 12–15, with an amendment: every geometric property a design asserts needs a gate or a labelled-assumed flag, as a fifth check at assertion time — the level the four scoring-layer rules don't reach. **Round 116 (Theseus)** reproduced Round 115's verifier live (36/36 self-checks) and executed the merge as **rule 16**, not a renumbered rule 12: `grep`ing the docs and scripts trees found 141 citations of rules 12–15 across 26 files, 66 of them in dated session logs and mail, which reusing 12 would have silently redefined. Old rules keep their numbers and full text with forward pointers to the five checks (16a–16e); a new verifier requires all of it present verbatim or the file goes red. Ran Round 115's own "list assertions, list gates, diff" procedure over the *whole* pre-registration rather than the one sentence that minted it — 11 asserted properties, 5 gates, 4 assumed-labels — and found the raw procedure returns noise until scoped to assertions that *support* a number (not ones that only ever weaken a claim). Scoped, it surfaces **two ungated supporting assertions, both in S-unexposed** — the cell Round 115's fix was copied *from*: "only one query productive" at arm scope (now gate 2b) and "the restriction is reachable only by `expand`" (now gate 3b). Neither is visible from the direction of Round 115's copy, because they're defects of the cell being copied from, not the cell being fixed — the fifth round now to find the previous round's correction reproduces one level up, and the first where the correction itself was sound. Neither moves any existing count (checked as set equality, not assumed) but both bear on the arm's search-volume hazard and the DV's meaning respectively. Own verifier caught itself failing 3 of 18 self-checks on first run — one a miscategorized property, two from a normaliser blind to markdown blockquotes, including the one checking that check 16a's own text survived the merge, which would have wrongly reported the merge as dropping the check it exists to enforce. **No GO requested by either round; `packages/` untouched; no live spend.** Docs: `docs/research/round115-the-ambiguity-was-a-missing-gate-not-a-missing-witness-2026-08-29.md`, `docs/research/round116-the-merge-is-executed-and-the-cell-you-copied-from-has-two-ungated-assertions-2026-08-29.md`.
- **Round 117–118, 2026-08-29 (both STOP fires, Daedalus then Theseus, zero live spend) — arm S-exposed's region count closes at 3 (not 2), then the check that closed it turns out unable to have gone red, and its closure was hostage to an open item that doesn't actually hold it up.** **Round 117 (Daedalus)** answered Theseus's two Round 116 rulings — gate 3b's scope adopted at both cells (on a stronger ground: at S-exposed, 3b is the only clause saying the restriction region isn't query-renderable, which is what makes the Round 115 gate-1b entailment stateable there at all); the polarity qualifier adopted with an amendment, since polarity is a relation to a *use*, not a fixed property of an assertion, so a use added after classification is invisible to it unless enforced by a marker-count-equals-classified-site-count check. Then found the third open item — S-exposed's region count — was **never open**: gate 3b already asserts no query renders the restriction row, so the restriction is a region outside the call-1 union, and the count is **3 by construction**, derivable from §1 and the DV alone, no corpus or second seat required. His own Round 115 §4 antecedent ("exactly two regions") was false when written; withdrawn, no number moves (still 10 surviving shapes). Correcting the antecedent deleted the sentence labelling the count assumed, which turned Theseus's `L-region` self-check red — a real gap in check 16a's data model (no branch for *retired*, only *gated* and *labelled-assumed*) — fixed. Also found his and Theseus's citation counts for the rule-16 merge don't reproduce (127/130/157/161 measured live vs. Theseus's reported 141/26/66) because the denominator moves with its corpus (rule 1); the merge argument stands, the number was replaced by the class in both the rules file and the verifier comment. **Round 118 (Theseus)** adopted both rulings as written and, doing the one item handed back to him, read §(f)'s code rather than its prose and found two defects in the section that did the closing. **(1)** The corrected-antecedent check was **vacuous**: `!queryRenderable.includes(r) && r.renderedBy === 'query'` conjoins a predicate with its own negation, true of nothing, so the arm's corrected central claim was reported satisfied by a tautology; the existing mutation missed it because it drove a different expression than the filter under test (rule 8 one level up: a mutation licenses only the assertion it actually runs through). Fixed by splitting the conflated fields; the 3b mutant now drives the filter to a non-empty set, the first demonstration it can go red. **(2)** The closure was tied to an open item in the *encoding*, not the prose: the docblock gives two independent grounds for the third region (gate 3b, and the DV needing somewhere for `expand` to go) but one field carried both, so a 3b-failing mutant would fell the DV ground as collateral, silently reopening a closed item "on the strength of a field layout." Separated with a mutation in each direction, blast radius written into the pre-registration before 3b is checked. **(3)** Split the as-of label by tense: a frozen claim over fixed ids (`{P4, P6u}`) and a live zero-ungated-and-unfixed count can't share one assertion, since a twelfth ungated property would make the label go red at "3" with the only green repair being to edit "as of Round 116" into a lie; added a third check asserting the two must disagree or the label is decorative. Against himself: named that reading a green verifier's prose and self-check names is not the same as reading the verifier — he'd called §(f) green last fire on exactly that basis. **No count moves** (region count 3, surviving shapes 10, four underived S-side pre-spend conditions unchanged). **No GO requested by either round; `packages/` untouched; no live spend.** Docs: `docs/research/round117-the-region-count-was-decided-by-the-design-not-open-2026-08-29.md`, `docs/research/round118-the-check-that-closed-the-item-could-not-have-gone-red-2026-08-29.md`.
